The source is [Repack] (`endBorrow`'s `repackCheck`, D64: a borrow of a value of a dependent type ends with its content checked against the type again), which opens an unknown for a lent part and keeps its type. Smallest: `DepFields.CellGet`, ending `c : &Cell = borrow_0 MkCell(Some(loan_1), ⋆)` (`Cell := MkCell(o : Opt(Word), h : IsSome(Word, o))`) while `r : &Word = borrow_1 σ2` is live: the counter goes from 4 to 5. The 21 are `DepFields.CellGet`, `GetLentOk` and nineteen of the hash map case studies. The adversarial probes: 275 ends, 49 re-runs, no growth. Measured, not kernel-checked (as D33's restatements): the counterexample states are the checker's recorded states, and transcribing one (its globals included) was not attempted.

**Decided (2026-11-21): `DeltaOk` asks `K.Δ` only at referenced unknowns** (`∀ σ, st.Refd σ → σ < st.absTy.size → K.Δ σ = deltaOf K ρ st σ`; moved from `Run.lean` to `Steps/Statements.lean`, beside `CtxOk`). The fixed-context statements `endBorrow_step_stmt` and `access_step_stmt` conclude the relation and `Good` only, which ask nothing of an unknown nothing names (`TypedAssignment.mem` and `Membership.absTy` were already confined to referenced unknowns, for `exitWorld`'s kept types). But `access_ctx` concluded `CtxOk K ρ` with `DeltaOk` asked at every typed unknown: after an end that keeps one, `K.Δ` there is whatever it was, so it was false at every context whose `Δ` differs there. With the clause confined it asks nothing of the kept unknown. `access_ctx` and `dropAtJoin_ctx` also gained `Good`: without it a state already naming the counter would name the kept unknown, with `K.Δ` free there. The other repair (the extension form for ends and accesses) was not needed for what was measured; an end that named a new unknown or added a record would need it, and none did. The reference reads `Δ` only at the unknowns of the terms and values it runs, all referenced, so the clause asks exactly what the run reads.

### D36. (WF6) bounds every unknown the state names; a record naming the counter (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; fixed)

(WF6)'s naming half bounded by the counter the unknowns of Ω's values, of Δ's types and of the goal, but not those of stored and deferred types or of the records, though its docstring said stored types were bounded. `Regress/FreshBound.lean` (built, standard axioms): `S` is `a : Nat ↦ 0` with no unknown typed, the counter at `0` and the generalisation record `0 := σ₀`; at `ρ₀` (everything `0`) the record's path condition holds and every clause as it stood is met (`goodOld_S`). A fresh unknown is the counter, `σ₀`, and `freshAbs_sim` concluded `Good` at `ρ.ext σ₀ v` for any `v`: at `v = 1` the record asks `1 = 0` at every `Δ'` (`not_good_ext`), so the statement was false at every context admitting `S` (`fresh_false`; `Nat` and `0` meet `GenTy`, `genTy_S`).

Fixed: (WF6) is now `(∀ σ, st.Refd σ → σ < size) ∧ (Δ's types bounded) ∧ (each [Split] refinement's unknown and value bounded) ∧ size = nextAbs ∧ (labels bounded)`: every unknown `MState.Refd` names (values, stored and deferred types, the goal, the generalisation and η records, the types of those) and every refinement's (`refs` is not in `Refd`, but `TypedAssignment.refs` reads it). `S` is excluded (`not_wf6`). `MState.refd_lt` proves the first part for a concrete state from its places. Recording a refinement (`refs_sim`, `refine_sim'`) now takes the record's bound (`hb`), which both callers have from the η record's (WF6) (`Good.eta_bound`). The sweep's mirror is restated to match (exactly: stored and deferred types, records, refinements added); rerun (2026-11-21, spine): no violation in the suite's 68,541 states or the probes' 512, every other clause as before, both planted clauses reported.

### D37. B's remainder: the tail flag, [Entry], the captures as a decision, a closure's capture access (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

The model half of D29 point 2 that was still missing, built as D29 designed it, with the team lead's two decisions of 2026-11-21 (the decisions' alignment is a field of `CtxOk`; D34's shape facts wait for the exits).

- **The tail flag.** The reference run is `runT tl hint t` (`Run.lean`); `run` is `runT false`, so every statement about a run in a non-tail position reads as before. Tail positions are exactly where the checker's `checkTail` recurses and its untyped runs carry `MState.tailNext`: a function's body (the `.fix` clause's body run), and from a tail, a `let`'s body, a sequence's tail, a match's arms, and the one arm of a match on a proof. `J`'s transported term and a sealed program's re-run are not tails. A constant's body is checked by `eval`, not `checkTail`, so it is not a tail either (`Program.lean` unchanged). The inductions over the run are generalised over the flag (`runT_rinv`, `runT_congrE`, `runT_substSame`, `runT_congrA`, `runT_hintIndep`), each with its non-tail corollary under the old name.
- **[Entry]** (`matchEntry`, `Machine.joinEnds`). At a match not in tail position and not on a proof, after the scrutinee's access for its tag: the accesses `movedPlace` makes while the block's captures are computed (the reference's lent-prefix access, `entryReroot`), then each capture accessed as data in the block's order (`entryCap`); the places moved at the join are read after the entry, as the checker's second `staticJoin` is. If the match's declared type is a borrow, after the arm and the join the result's label is lent, as fills, the places the block takes by `&` and the sites the block's by-move borrow variables had before the arm (`entrySnapshot`, `entryLend`: `joinEnds`' reserved label and its marks). `J` does its transported term's entry, runs it, and drops at D84's join for the `J` term (`Machine.evalCore`'s `J`).
- **The captures as a decision.** `Decisions.blockCaps sc entry mt`: the places `movedPlace` is asked of (before re-rooting) and the captures with their modes, in `blockEntry`'s order. Its instance (`BlockCaps.staticBlockCaps`, in `checkerDecisions`) is `Machine.blockCaptures` K1(a)–(g) clause by clause with each input the checker reads from a value replaced by what the declarations say. Static already: the moved set (D84), the closures' captures and copy-ness (D80), the occurrence kinds, the maximal places, the covering by moved places, the sort order. Read from values by the checker, replaced: `movedPlace`'s re-rooting (to the shortest strict prefix moved at the match's start, by the entry set, or a proof, by the `.prop` reading; its other branches leave the place existing), K1(b)'s borrow-variable test (the `.ref` reading; D90: the value disjunct is redundant), K1(f)'s proof flag (the `.prop` reading), K1(g)'s split (only one-constructor values are split, so the constructor and its fields are the declaration's). That this equals `blockCaptures` at the checker's states is D90's obligation 1; it is the `blockCaps` field of the alignment below. On the concrete check's state the two agree (`#guard`).
- **The decisions' alignment is a field of `CtxOk`** (`DecAligned`, `Steps/Statements.lean`; the team lead's decision: assumed and concluded by every step, never a separate hypothesis). Wherever the checker's own decision procedure answers at the state's tables in a scope of readings (`scopeState st sc`), the context's decision in that scope gives the same answer; copy-ness reads no frame; the captures are asked at the top frame, at the places then moved (`entryOf`), and of every state the same up to what no decision reads. The separate hypothesis `hD : K.D = checkerDecisions st fuel` is gone from the five statements that had it, and all eight `*_aligned` theorems (`Steps/Decisions.lean`) are now PROVED from the field, given the induction hypothesis's `scopeOf_sstate` and two named simulation gaps (`jdMoves_sim`, `typeState_sim`: D84's functions are outside `DecideScope.decideSim`'s seventeen, its docstring's first gap). `DecideScope.EnvRel` now ignores the move log and the refinements (the simulation proof still goes through, so no decision reads either), which lets the field pass through the steps that write only those (`DecAligned.of_sameDec`; `logEffect_rel` and `refs_sim` stay proved).
- **A closure's capture access.** Forming a closure accesses each captured variable, oldest binding first, then moves it unless its declared reading is copy (`capStep`, `capOrder`; `Machine.capture`).
- **The concrete check** (`Regress/EntryCheck.lean`, built): `NT (a : Nat) : Unit := (let r = &a; let k = S(Z); match k { Z => (a := 1; ()), S _ => () }; *r := 2; ())` is rejected (the entry ends `r` though the arm that runs does not touch `a`), `NTOk` (the write first) and `NTTail` (the match in tail position, its other arm writing `*r`) are accepted. On the checker's own state at the match (recorded, `Config.recordStates`): the static captures and the checker's are the same (`a` by `&`, `k` read); the checker's entry, and its whole match, leave nothing live, `r` holding nothing, no site; the reference's entry on the related state does the same (`ref_entry`, kernel-checked: no live label, `r` holds nothing, `0` has no site), so the relation's holders and sites clauses hold afterwards with the identity renaming (the meanings are not computable); without the entry (a tail) the reference leaves `r` live (`ref_no_entry_tail`).
- **Residual: [Entry] at a field read** (removed in the checker by main's D93; see D38). The checker runs a term whose place operand has a field step `q.i` (a read, a borrow, an assignment) as the one-arm match on `q` that names the field (D81 [Proj], `projIn`, `projMatch`, then `evalMatchInd` with the term's tail flag), so in a non-tail position it has an entry whose block captures `q` whole: reading `x.0` ends a borrow of `x.1` (D90's known gap F4). The reference reads the field directly, so after such a read the two differ on which borrows are live. Not modelled: main's paused one-constructor lane (`eta-one-ctor`) would make such a match refine instead of close off, with no entry; the team lead to decide whether the reference models the current checker or waits.

### D38. Aligned with main's D93: a one-constructor match has no entry and no join (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

Main's D93 (ca91d72b, the team lead's answer to D37's residual: change the checker, not the model): in a typed run a match on a value of a type with one constructor (a projection read, borrow or assignment, a destructuring `let`, an explicit one-arm match) never closes off. An unknown scrutinee is refined in place (`etaInPlace`), a sealed one generalised first; the one arm runs directly, in tail position too; such a match has no entry and no join on any path. A stuck block refines each capture's one-constructor prefix in place after its entry and before its arms (`etaPlace`), refusing a fill over a live borrow.

- **The reference's match.** `.matchInd p n arms` decides it by the match's own declaration, as `Machine.evalMatchInd` does (`(← lookupInd ty).ctors.length == 1`): `oneCtor K.Γ n` (`Run.lean`), aligned through `CtxOk.inds` (`K.Γ = st.inds`), not a new field of `Decisions`. Such a match has no entry (`matchEntry`'s flag) and no join. The reference reads the tag of the scrutinee's meaning, which for an unknown of a one-constructor type is that constructor, so it runs the one arm as the checker does after refining in place; the refinement is a step of the checker alone, in the simulation form below. Matches on numbers have two constructors and are unchanged. The direct field read the reference always had is now the checker's too: the projection's match accesses `x` for its tag (sites at `x` itself only), then its arm reads the field, which is the reference's access of `x` at the field's path.
- **No projection entry**, as the team lead directed.
- **The block's in-place refinement.** `Decisions.blockCaps` and the entry need nothing new: `blockCaptures` is unchanged, `etaPlace` runs after `entryAccess` and makes no access (it refuses a fill over a live borrow, which the entry has ended). The reference never forms a block and never refines, so the relation needs the refinement as a step in the simulation form: the reference untouched, the assignment and Δ extended at the fields named. `etaInPlace_sim` (`Steps/MovedPlace.lean`) is that step for one unknown, ASSEMBLED from `etaRecorded_sim`, `etaFresh_sim` and `refine_sim'` (no new `sorry`); it serves `evalMatchInd`'s in-place refinement, `movedPlace`'s η branch and the block's. `etaPlace_sim` (the block's whole refinement, recursive like `movedPlace`) is stated, open, for `closeBlock_step_stmt`.
- **What D93 changed in the drop's proof.** Little. The join no longer runs at a one-constructor match, so `joinDrop_step` is used only at matches with several constructors (and on numbers); nothing in the drop's proof depended on a one-constructor match having one. `movedPlace`'s η branch is still used: by the join at other matches (a dropped place below a one-constructor prefix, which the grain allows) and by `movedPlace`'s callers in the block's capture computation. The checker's `movedPlace` now calls `etaInPlace`; the copy `mpTail` follows it, and the η branch of `movedPlace_step` is now one call of `etaInPlace_sim` (the assembly still has no `sorry` in its body). `GrainOk`, the exits and the other branches are unchanged.
- **Verdicts the rebase flipped**, each accepted on main since D93 and now asserted so: `AdequacyProbes2.Disjoint` (a field read while another field is lent: rejected under D90's F4, accepted again, as the model always read it), `OpenWriteDep.OW` and `OWM` (an index rewritten at an unknown `w`: the projection's match no longer closes off with a stuck arm returning `w` open; `w` stays open after it, as `OWK`'s, and nothing after relies on its type).
- **The sweeps on the rebased tree** (2026-11-21): suite 69,949 states, 2,162 verdicts as expected, every real clause holds; probes 538 states, one clause failed, (WF12) in D33's form (D39, restated; rerun clean); `--ends`: 25,430 ends from 74,831 recorded states, 1,219 touching a re-run, 5,569 failed or out of fuel, 399 leaving more unknowns (22 declarations, all with typing on, none adding a record, none naming the unknown after), probes 328 ends, none.
- **The concrete checks**, on the rebased tree: `StaticLend` (the reborrow that outlives its borrow, at an unknown and a known `n`; Pick at `Z`) and `EntryCheck` (a non-tail match on a number) pass unchanged. The fourth (`Regress/OneCtorEntry.lean`, built): `let r = &x.1; let y = clone(x.0); *r := Z; ()` is accepted on a known pair (`KP`) and on a parameter (`PP`, refined in place by the borrow). On the checker's own state before the read, its evaluation of `clone(x.0)` leaves `0` live, held by `r`, with its one site at `x`'s field `1` (`#guard`, both); the reference's read of `x.0` on the related state does the same (`ref_read_keeps`, kernel-checked), so the relation's holders and sites clauses hold afterwards; the entry D93 removed (an access of `x` whole) would have ended it (`ref_whole_ends`).
- The four core lemmas are re-proved on the rebased tree (standard axioms).

### D39. (WF12) restated a second time: two live fills at one place (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; fixed)

The rerun of the sweep's probes on the rebased tree (D38) failed one real clause: (WF12) in D33's form ("at a position holding a live fill, no other live borrow has a site at the fill's path or below it"), in 3 states of `LentUnder.TwoParts`, which main's D93 made accepted (Rust accepts it): `let r = Whole(&c); let a = IdN(&(*r).0); let b = &(*r).1; *a := 3; *b := 4; r := &e; let d = c; d`. Two disjoint parts of the returned borrow `r`'s place are lent onward, to `a` and `b`; when `r` is re-pointed its borrow ends, and its mark at `c` passes to EVERY live borrow its content was lent onward to (D90's [End]), so both are filled at `c` whole: `c = lent_2 (lent_4 (lent_2 loan_2, loan_4))`. Each fill has the other borrow's site at its own path. Kernel-checked in `Regress/TwoFills.lean` (built: the state's shape violates D33's form, `not_wf12D33`; the probe's own run reaches two fills at one place, `#guard`). Not a checker fault: reading `c.0` ends both (both marks stand at `c`), on both paths.

Restated (`WF.lean`) to what the absorption of the lent prefix needs (`AccessAbsorb.AbsorbInv`'s second half), and what both states meet: **under a live fill, only borrows filled there**: at a position holding a live borrow's fill at a path, every live borrow with a site at that path or below it has a fill at that same path. D33's form is the record (and D29's before it). `lentPrefix_fill` is re-proved from it (shorter: the other borrow's fill at the lent prefix is given directly). The sweep's mirror is restated, with D33's form counted as a premise. Rerun: suite 69,949 states (2,162 verdicts as expected), no violation; a live fill in 1,825 states, D33's form never violated there; probes 538 states, no violation; a live fill in 224, another live borrow with a site at a live fill's position in 18, D33's form violated in 3 (all `TwoParts`). Read off the checker, NOT proved preserved.

### D40. The shape facts are a field of `CtxOk`; the sweep's gap closed (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

D34's facts are `Steps.ShapeFacts` (`Steps/Statements.lean`), the field `CtxOk.shapes`, by the lead's criterion: every step assumes them and every step's `CtxOk` conclusion owes them, as for the aligned decisions (`CtxOk.dec`). They are stated at every place in scope that exists, by the checker's own declared reading (`Decisions.placeDecl` at the top frame's readings, `DecideScope.topReadings`, which `rel_scopeOf` shows is the reference's scope): (U) an unknown at a place declared an inductive of the declarations has that inductive's type; (V) such a place holds a constructor value of it or a stand-in (`Value.fitsInd`); (R) a place declared a borrow type holds a borrow, `⊥`, a loan or a mark over one (`Value.refFits`); (P) `placeType?` of a sealed program at a place declared an inductive is that inductive, asked of every state equal up to the place log and the records (`SameDec`), as `blockCaps` is; (A) every constructor value of a declared inductive has its constructor's number of fields (`arityBad`); (M) every mark outside sealed programs and code is of a live borrow; (L) every loan and mark in Ω is of a live borrow. `ShapeFacts.of_sameDec` carries them across a step that writes only the log.

The gap the lead named (the sweep tested bindings and fields with a written type; the clause quantifies over every place in scope) is closed by extending the mirror: `Sweep.lean`'s `shapeFacts` clause enumerates every place under every top-frame variable that exists, reads its declared reading by the checker's `placeDecl`, and tests (U), (V), (R), (P), (A), (M) and (L) there. Rerun: no violation in the suite's 69,949 states or the probes' 538. Premises in the suite: 46,076 places declared an inductive, 42,841 holding an unknown, 8,046 holding a sealed program, 26,902 below a field or `*` step, 21,057 declared a borrow type (probes: 334, 60, 35, 142, 304).

The field count (A) does not follow from how constructor values are built, as far as the proof can see: `Good`'s membership admits an open constructor value with the wrong number of fields (D32's `OpenIn`), and every builder (a constructor term, an η-refinement, a generic value, a refinement's substitution) would need its own preservation proof. It stays a read-off clause, swept, owed by every step like the others.

The exits of `movedPlace` are proved on them (standard axioms): an unknown or a sealed program at the parent has a one-constructor type (`exit_abs` by (U), `exit_sealed` by (P); through `*`, (R) excludes both), and a parent through which the place is missing, with no live fill, unknown or sealed program, holds `⊥` or `⋆` (`exit_other_missing`, by (V), (A), (L), (R)). For this the grain is restated per step (`StepOk`): a field step through a declared one-constructor inductive AT AN INDEX ITS CONSTRUCTOR HAS, a `*` step through a declared borrow type (D41 on what provides it).

### D41. `movedPlace` re-roots to a parent where the reference drops nothing (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; open, proposed to main)

Proving `exit_other` found two cases where the checker's join drops a parent whole and the reference drops the place (or nothing). Both are recorded on the checker's own runs.

1. **The place exists after its parent's re-rooting** (`Regress/ReRootExists.lean`). `movedPlace q` re-roots the parent `p` first. When a strict prefix of `p` holds a live fill, that inner call accesses the prefix whole (ending the borrow, which gives the prefix its content back) and asks for `p` again, which now exists; the outer call does not ask for `q` again, and if `p` holds a constructor value it returns `p`. On the checker's state at the end of an arm that lends `c` whole (`rr := Whole(&c)`), `movedPlace((c.0).0)` returns `c.0`, holding `MkIn(σ0, σ1)`, and `(c.0).0` exists. The reference accesses `c` whole and drops `(c.0).0`. The join does not reach this case there: the arm uses `c` whole, so the join moves `c` whole (K1(g)); a lending before the match is ended by the entry's access of the captured place; no accepted program is known on which the join reaches it. But `movedPlace_step_stmt` is about every place at the grain, and its leaf `reRoot_created` is false as stated.
2. **An ill-typed place in an arm the checker does not run** (`Regress/UnrunArmPlace.lean`). A match on a known constructor runs only its arm; the others are read for their moves and never checked, so a place there can be `(c.0).5` (`In` has two fields) or `*(c.0)` (`c.0` is no borrow). `jdMoves` keeps it (`jdGrain` checks only that a field step is through a one-constructor type), and `movedPlace` re-roots it to `c.0`, which the join drops whole; the reference's drop of a place that does not exist does nothing. On the accepted `BadIdx` the drop's statement is false with the grain as first stated. The checker is the conservative side: `BadIdxRead` and `BadDerefRead` (which read `(c.0).1` afterwards) are rejected, `GoodIdxRead` (a well-typed move of `(c.0).0`) accepted. With the grain restated (D40), the statement may hold, but the restated grain's index and `*` clauses are not what `jdMoves` provides, so the caller cannot discharge it there.

**Proposed to main, one rule for both:** `movedPlace` returns the place itself wherever it does not make it exist (its drop is then a no-op, as the reference's is), keeping only the exit after a fill the access does not clear. It changes no verdict where the place is missing under `⊥` or `⋆` (both drops are no-ops) or where the grain holds (the other exits are then unreachable, `exit_abs`, `exit_sealed`); it would accept `BadIdxRead` and `BadDerefRead`, as it already accepts `BadIdx`: the checker does not check an arm it does not run, by design, and that arm never runs (Rust rejects all four, being ill-typed). The model then needs neither `reRoot_created` nor the grain's index and `*` clauses, and `MPOut`'s second disjunct goes. Until decided, `reRoot_created` is a named `sorry` marked false, `exit_other` is assembled on it, and the rest of the exits are proved.

### D42. The reference's drop tests a holder first, as the checker does (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; fixed)

`holder_follow` (the holder of a borrow the drop ends is not `moved` or `⋆` in the reference) was false as stated: moving out through a borrow is allowed if the content is whole again when the borrow ends (D53 (h)), so the checker reaches `r ↦ borrow_ℓ ⊥` (`Regress/HolderMoved.lean`: `let r = &a; let x = *r; *r := b; x`, accepted, the state recorded between the move and the write). The reference's meaning of a holder is its content's final meaning, which reads `moved` there, and `Run.dropAt` tested the meaning before the holder, so it did nothing where the checker's `dropAtJoin`, which tests its stored value, a borrow, ends it (`endBorrow` rejects a holed content outside an observation and ends it inside one, D68). Fixed in the reference, not by a hypothesis: `dropAt` tests the holder first, by its shape (the relation's holders are the checker's), and only then the meaning. `holder_follow` is deleted; the borrow case of `dropOne_exists` is now immediate, and its `⊥`/`⋆` case shows the root is no holder from `SimEq`'s holders. Seven proofs that split `dropAt` were repaired (`Mono`, `RunPres`, `RunEnv`, `RunOrd`, `JoinDropLib`); the four core lemmas are unchanged (standard axioms).

### D43. (WF14): no live borrow is lent strictly above a moved part (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`lentPrefix_none_of_noop` (where the checker's dropped place is `⊥` its drop does nothing, so the reference's access of the lent prefix must do nothing too) was a fact read off the checker and swept as clause (iii), and no clause of `Good`, so it could not be proved. It is now (WF14) (`WF.lean`), in exactly the sweep mirror's form: where a position's value has `⊥` at a path, no live borrow has a site at that position at a strict prefix of the path, compared by `pathKey` as `lentPrefix` compares. [Borrow] rejects a partly moved place, and a move under a fill reaches the fill first and ends it. `lentPrefix_none_of_noop` is PROVED from it (standard axioms) through the relation's live labels and sites. Rerun on main's tree: suite 69,949 states (2,162 verdicts as expected), no violation; a `⊥` part at a position holding a live fill in 32 states, a `⊥` or `⋆` part strictly under a mark in 19; probes 538 states, no violation. Read off the checker, NOT proved preserved. `lentPrefix_none_of_proof` (the `⋆` case) stays open: a non-copy place holding `⋆` under a live fill did not arise.

### D44. The reference's reading of a place at the grain is the checker's value's meaning (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`content_follow` is PROVED (standard axioms), from a lemma about every place at the join's grain (`Steps/PlaceTyping.lean`, `followMean`): the reference's `Sem.follow` along the place's path, from the meaning of its position, is the meaning (at some hint) of the value the checker reaches, or `⋆`. Each step is `step_mean`: through `*` the place is declared a borrow type and holds a borrow (R); through a field the place is declared a one-constructor inductive of the declarations and holds a constructor value of it with its fields (V, A), recording its parameters (WF10), whose meaning is the constructor value describing itself (`ctorM_dat`) with each field's meaning (`meanFields_get`), or `⋆` if the declaration is `Prop`-sorted; marks are transparent on both sides. The place helpers moved into that module from `MovedPlace.lean` and `DropOneAsm.lean`.

Two restatements, in the open. `content_follow` asked `moved` exactly of a `⊥`: false inside a value of a `Prop`-sorted declaration, whose meaning is `⋆` whatever its fields hold; it now says `moved` or `⋆`, both of which make the reference's drop do nothing, as the checker's does (the only use). And it lacked the grain and the typing: without them a step of the reference's reading need not be the checker's (a constructor value of an undeclared type means `err`). And (V) now says a mark sits over a value that fits (`Value.fitsInd` recurses through a mark; it allowed a mark over anything), which the proof needs at a lent place and which the sweep confirms (rerun with the mirror changed to match: no violation at any place in scope, suite 69,949 states, probes 538).

### D45. The write of `⊥` beside sealed programs (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`botWrite_rel` (L3's relation half, proved) asked the written value to hold no sealed program at all (`RecPlain`), so that the later fields of a constructor, typed at the earlier ones, mean the same after the write. The sweep's new clause (H) counts a sealed program in a constructor value's field in 1,709 of the suite's states (a sealed program anywhere in Ω in 21,336), so `botWrite_hyps`, which owed `RecPlain`, was false there. `mean_updAt_bot_sealed` is PROVED (standard axioms) and `botWrite_rel` now asks only what it needs: no bare constructor, and no sealed program whose tail reads the hint (`Value.topHintFree`), under the run's code meaning, where such a value means the same at every hint (`HintIndep.meanV_hintIndep`; `meanK_hintFree`, `topHintFree_of`, BotWrite's `meanFields_setH`). That every sealed program in Ω has a hint-free tail is (H), swept: no violation, suite 69,949 states, probes 538 (71 with a sealed program). A candidate, read off the checker ([Close]'s programs end in a read), not yet a clause: `botWrite_hyps` owes it.

### D46. (WF15): no recorded reading holds a loan; `sealed_loanFree` proved (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`sealed_loanFree` (a sealed program in Ω with no live hole holds no loan, which the generalisation of a sealed program needs) could not follow from (L): the checker's `Value.loans` does not walk the declared types a closure or Π-type records (`rd`, D82), and `Value.hasLoan` (an `anyAtom`) does, so a loan there would be invisible to `liveLoansIn` (hence to `fillHoles`) and visible to `hasLoan` (hence to `sitesOf`). Swept as (RD): no recorded reading holds a loan or a mark, suite 69,949 states (a closure or Π-type with a recorded reading in 3,536), probes 538 (20). It is now (WF15) (`WF.lean`), (WF11)'s kind of fact. With it, `hasLoan` is `loans` (`SitesFollow.mem_loans_of_hasLoan`, the converse of `anyAtom_of_mem_loans` outside recorded readings, a mutual induction over values and terms), and `sealed_loanFree` is PROVED (standard axioms): a loan the program holds is in its loans, so it is live by (L), so it is a live hole, and there is none. It now takes the run context. Read off the checker, NOT proved preserved.

### D47. What the write of `⊥` needs at the drop, proved (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`botWrite_hyps` (`botWrite_rel`'s hypotheses at the drop's data case) is PROVED (standard axioms) from three more facts read off the checker and swept, and the typing of places along the grain:
- (WF16), first (H): every sealed program in Ω has a hint-free tail (D45). Now a clause of `Good`.
- (N), `ShapeFacts.ctors`: every constructor value in Ω is of a data declaration of the state's declarations, not named `Nat` (a `Prop`-sorted declaration's values are `⋆`, `Machine.evalCtor`, D42; numbers are `zero`/`succ`). Swept: no violation, suite 69,949 states (28,428 with a constructor value), probes 538.
- (Ty), `ShapeFacts.typeRoot`: a variable of the top frame holding a type value (a `Π` or an inductive type) is declared a sort, so a copy, which the join never drops whole. Swept: no violation, suite (89 states with such a variable), probes 538.
The write's path conditions (`NatAlong`) hold along every place at the grain where the write's path exists (`PlaceTyping.natAlong_grain`, by `natAlong_node` at each prefix: a `*` through a declared borrow holding a borrow, a field of a declared one-constructor data inductive holding its constructor, by (V), (R), (A), (N)). The root is no holder: the reference's is none after its accesses (shapes only shrink) and `SimEq` takes a checker holder to a reference one. The statement lost two unused hypotheses and gained the run context, the copy decision and the reference's shape at the root, all at hand where it is used.

### D48. The checker's access clears the place, read through the relation (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`access_clears` (after the checker's access of the dropped place, no live borrow has a site at, over or under it) was stated about the checker's two loops' exits: `accessPath` leaves no live loan on the path, `accessInside` none in the content, but the second's ends could in principle disturb the first's result, which needs a frame lemma for the checker's [End] (what an end leaves untouched: substitution only where the loan occurs, marks passed only where the ended borrow's marks were, re-runs only of programs holding its hole). It is restated and PROVED (standard axioms) through the relation instead: the reference state related to the checker's after its access is an access of the same position and path (`lentPrefix_absorbed`), at its fixpoint no live label is reached there (`SState.access_post`), and the relation's live labels (`rel_live_ref'`, moved to `DropOne.lean`) and sites (`Rel.sites`) are the checker's. The frame lemma for [End] is still what `access_ctx` and the [End] step will need.

### D49. No `⋆` the join drops below a variable; `lentPrefix_none_of_proof` proved (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`lentPrefix_none_of_proof` (no lent prefix above a `⋆` the join drops) was open because its premise never arose. The sweep's (C⋆) first asked that every place in scope holding `⋆` be declared a copy type, and found it false at a variable: one declared a borrow of a proposition holds `⋆` and is no copy (`Universes.BorrowQ`, `let b = &r` with `r` a proof; 6,447 suite states in 69 declarations). At a variable the statement holds anyway: there is no strict prefix to be lent. Restricted to places below a variable, (C⋆) holds (`ShapeFacts.proofCopy`: such a place is declared a copy type, a field of a proof): no violation, suite 69,949 states (4,007 with `⋆` below a variable), probes 538. `lentPrefix_none_of_proof` is PROVED (standard axioms) from it, with the run context and the variable's position in place of the resolution. Read off the checker, NOT proved preserved.

### D50. `Good` after the write of `⊥`, proved (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`botWrite_good` (L3's other half: `Good` after the checker's write of `⊥` at the dropped place) is PROVED (standard axioms, `Steps/BotWriteGood.lean`). Its statement gained what the proof needs and the drop has: the position, a root written is no holder, the access cleared the place (`hclear`), and `botWrite_rel`'s value conditions. The typed assignment is untouched and the unknowns named are fewer (`refd_setAtE`); the stored types are unchanged (`bind_setAtE`, `posTyM_setAtE`); the written position's meaning is the old with a part moved out (`movedOf_mean_updAt`, with `MovedOf` transitive) and partial membership survives that (`pmem_moved_part`); every clause of well-formedness survives the removal of a part: atom tests by their shallowness (`ShallowAtom` instances), the loans outside sealed programs a sublist (`outerLoans_updAt_bot`, so each still occurs at most once), the borrows held, their liveness and their sites unchanged after the access, a borrow after the write the same borrow before (`borrow_updAt_bot`, for (WF3)), and a `⊥` after the write an old one or the written one (`follow_updAt_bot`, for (WF14), with `hclear` for the new one). With it, the data case of the drop of one place rests only on `dropAt_data` and the run context's preservation (`access_ctx`), which wait on the two design questions reported (the reference's data test, and decisions across cache and unknown growth).

### D51. Aligned with main's D94: `movedPlace` keeps the place, or rejects (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

Main's D94 (8035f7ed) settled D41 in the checker: `movedPlace` returns the place whenever it exists after refining, generalising and accessing (D41's first finding: the place is dropped), a prefix holding `⊥` or `⋆` for a no-op, the exit after a fill the access did not end as it was, and otherwise rejects the definition with a [Join] error (D41's second finding: `BadIdx` is rejected). Spine is rebased onto it. The reference's `mpTail` mirrors the new code (tied by `rfl` through `Ochr.movedPlace.eq_def`; the pattern equation lemma times out on the larger body); `mpTail'` writes each fall-through to the last part out (`mpTail_eq`, `rfl`); the last part is `mpFinal`, and `mpFinal_inv` says it changes nothing and returns the place where it exists or a `⊥`/`⋆` prefix, the rejection being no run. So no exit lemma is left: `exit_abs`, `exit_sealed`, `exit_other` and `exit_other_missing` are deleted, and `reRoot_created` (false) and `reRoot_missing` with them. With them went what only the exits read: the grain's index and `*` clauses (`StepOk` is `jdGrain`'s condition again: a field step through a one-constructor inductive of the declarations), `arity_one`, and `ShapeFacts`' (U), (R), (P), (A) and (M); `followMean` and `natAlong_grain` now read a step's constructor and field from the step's existence. `ShapeFacts` keeps (V), (L), (N), (Ty), (C⋆). `Regress/UnrunArmPlace`'s `BadIdx` is a rejected row; `Regress/ReRootExists` checks that `movedPlace` now returns `(c.0).0` itself.

Sweeps on the rebased tree: suite 70,207 states, 2,173 verdicts as expected, every real clause holds; probes 538 states, every clause holds; `--ends` 25,451 ends run (1,219 touching a sealed program holding the loan, 5,569 failed or out of fuel), 399 after which the state has more unknowns, in 22 declarations, with no record added and no new unknown referenced after; `--ends --probes` 328 ends, none growing.

### D52. The lead's (B): the reference's drop does not read `moved` (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

The reference must not decide an effect by reading a meaning where the checker decides it by the stored value. `Run.dropAt` no longer tests `moved`: a place the reference reads, no holder and no copy, is accessed and written `moved`. Where the checker's place is `⊥` (its drop does nothing) this is a no-op for the relation, PROVED (standard axioms): the access ends nothing (`clear_of_bot`: over the place by (WF14); at or under it no site at all, since nothing along a site's own path is `⊥`, `SitesFollow.site_not_bot`) and the write of `moved` over `moved` is the write of `⊥` over `⊥` (`updAt_bot_self`, `setAtE_self_bind`), which `botWrite_rel` relates. The test for a proof stays: a variable can hold `⋆` under a reading that is no copy (a proof captured by a closure, inside the closure's body, `Snapshots.CapP2`; a borrow of a proposition, `Universes.BorrowQ`, rejected), where the checker's drop does nothing and nothing static tells the reference; the meaning there is exactly `⋆`'s. `dropAt_data` stays open and narrower: after the prefix's access the reference reads the place, and not as a proof, where the checker holds data (values of propositions are `⋆` in the checker, P5). The `Id`-observation case (D68: an end over a moved-out content succeeds only inside an observation's final ends) has no direct run: outside those ends the checker rejects the drop of a lender whose borrower moved its content out; inside them only re-runs of sealed programs could reach a join.

### D53. The decisions read only their core of the state; growth of the unknowns is conditional (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built, one finding)

`DecideScope.EnvRel` is restated to what the decision procedures read: Σ (`globals`, `inds`), Δ (`absTy`), the reading mode (`liveScope`), the top frame's readings, and, as a separate and clearly marked field, the two caches they write into the state (`constDecls`, `classCache`), which main is measuring for removal; nothing is proved about their coherence. The simulation `decideSim` goes through unchanged, so the env's values, the counters, the records (`refs`, `neutrals`, `etaRefs`), the goal and the move log are all free. `DecAligned.of_core` transfers the decisions to any state with the same core (`DecCore`) and the same top frame's readings, the block's captures, which read values (D90's first obligation), asked separately. The two named gaps of the alignment are closed: `jdMoves_sim` and `typeState_sim` are PROVED (standard axioms) by the same induction over `jdMoves`' and `jdAssigns`' mutual blocks (`jdSim`, `jaSim`, `sim_typeState`).

Growth of the unknowns is not covered, and cannot be in the form asked. The decision procedures read Δ at the unknowns of the values they inspect (`valueDecl (.abs σ)`, `isPropV`, `sealedSort?` call `absType σ`). So two states whose Δ differ only by new unknowns decide alike on every input whose unknowns exist in the older one (a one-sided simulation: what the older state decides, the newer decides the same), but not on inputs naming a new unknown, where the older state's run fails. `CtxOk.dec` asks the decisions of the run context, read once, to be the checker's on every input at every state; a sealed program built after they were read embeds values with newer unknowns (a stuck call's arguments), and its re-run asks the decisions about it. As stated, `CtxOk.dec` is then not preserved by a step that creates unknowns (399 of the suite's 25,451 ends do, D35, and every refinement and generalisation does). With the lead.

### D54. The reference's drop reads the place after its access (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`dropAt` read the place at the state after the lent prefix's access and before the place's own. No checker state is related to that state: the checker reads the place, then accesses it, and the reference had already accessed the lent prefix. Relating it needed a lemma that an access leaves every non-holder's reading at the closed future unchanged, with a side condition that the place's own root never holds a label the access ends (a holder could be ended by a relend chain, which only an order on sites excludes). The drop now reads the place as `contentOf` and `tagOf` do, after its [Access] at the closed future, and keeps the access only where it then writes; every branch's result is unchanged. In the data case the reading is at the reference state related to the checker's after its access, where `followMean` applies; in the `⊥` and `⋆` cases the access ends nothing (`clear_of_bot`; a `⋆` at a root lends nothing, `clear_of_proof_root`; a `⋆` below a variable is a copy by (C⋆)). Seven split proofs were made robust to the binding (`dsimp only; repeat' split`).

`dropAt_data` is PROVED from three hypotheses at the drop (no holder at a root, the reading after the access exists and is not `⋆`, no copy). The reading's hypothesis is `dataRead`, PROVED from `followMean` (whose `⋆` disjunct now records that the checker holds `⋆` too; `step_mean` takes (N)) and one new obligation, `dataMean_ne_proof`: the meaning of what the checker holds at a non-copy place, other than `⋆`, is not `⋆` (typing: `⋆` is a member of propositions only, a place read as a proposition is a copy, a borrow of a proposition holds `⋆` itself). The checker's content after its access must not be `⋆` either: below a variable (C⋆) gives it; at a root it is `access_root_not_proof` (new): the access substitutes a loan by its borrow's content and re-runs sealed programs, and nothing should make data a proof. Swept as clause (D⋆), the syntactic shadow of both (no non-copy place holds an unknown or sealed program of a proposition, or a loan whose borrow holds `⋆`): no violation on 70,207 suite states and 538 probe states; the premise "a loan whose borrow holds `⋆`" never arises.

### D55. (Hη): every η record is top-hint-free; `etaRecorded_sim` proved (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

Refining an unknown substitutes its recorded η-refinement wherever the unknown occurs, so the refinement must mean the unknown at every future and hint. `Good` gives it at the unknown's type and the constant future; (WF11) gives no loan, so every future (`meanV_congr`); for every hint the record must not be a bare constructor of a declaration with parameters, which nothing stated. It is a new field of the checker's typing, `ShapeFacts.etaHint` (Hη), swept first: 11,769 suite states hold an η record (10,442 of a declaration with parameters), 52 probe states, no violation. `etaRecorded_sim` is PROVED with it (and now takes the context).

### D56. The drop where `movedPlace` re-rooted to `⊥` or `⋆`, proved but for one typing fact (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built)

`joinDropOne_noop` concludes the relation (what `dropOne_step_asm` uses) and is PROVED from `grain_deref_not_proof`. The checker does nothing at the prefix `q'`. Nothing is lent over, at or under `q'` (`clear_of_bot`; a `⋆` at a root lends nothing), so neither the lent prefix's access nor the place's own ends anything (`unreached_of_clear`, `lentPrefix_none_of_unreached`, `dataHit_of_prefix`). The reference reads the place through `q'`: `⋆` reads `⋆` along any path, and it does nothing; `moved` reads nothing through a field step, and it does nothing; `moved` along `*` steps reads `moved`, and it writes `moved` along the place, which is the write along `q'` (`sem_updAt_append_derefs`) over a moved part, related to the unchanged checker state as in D52 (`botWrite_hyps_ty`: `botWrite_hyps` with the root's type-value fact as a hypothesis, since `q'` need not be a non-copy place).

A `⋆` strictly below a variable above a dropped place is excluded: through field steps the place would hold `⋆` and be a copy by (C⋆), which the join does not drop; through a `*` it is `grain_deref_not_proof` (open): the join derefs no proof below a variable, since the arm that moves the place is typed, `*` of a non-borrow is rejected, and a `⋆` below a variable is a copy, which no borrow type is. The grain no longer carries the `*` step's borrow type (D51), so this is stated on its own. The general fact "no live site above a `⋆`" is false (19 suite states, the (WF14) clause's first premise), so it is not a clause.

### D57. The lead's (ii): decisions at an extended table, stated not built; two findings (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; stated)

The lead's decision on D53: a step that extends Δ extends the context's decisions to the checker's at the extended table, so `CtxOk.dec` holds at the new context by construction; what must not be disturbed is a one-sided transfer (on inputs naming only old unknowns the answer is unchanged) and a congruence of the run in the decisions, the latter stated and not proved. `Steps/DecExtend.lean` states them and nothing builds on them yet: `Decisions.AgreeOld n D D'` (a place's and a reading's decisions name no unknown, so they always agree), `decAligned_grow` (the transfer, for a step that only grows the table, `Grows`, the caches a marked hypothesis), `runT_congrD` and `meanV_congrD` (the congruence, the analogue of `runT_congrA`).

Two findings before building on it. (1) The growth is not only at the steps whose statements already extend the context: D53's 399 ends are ends of borrows, and `endBorrow_step_stmt`, `access_step_stmt`, `access_ctx`, `dropAtJoin_ctx` and the refinement's leaves all conclude at the same context, so carrying the new decisions reaches every one of them, with the congruence to move the relation and `Good` across. (2) A refinement rewrites the old entries of the table (`Env.mapState` with its types, after `substEnv … true`), and the decisions read an unknown's type through `absType` (`valueDecl`, `sortOf?`, `isPropV`), so the one-sided transfer, stated for a table whose old entries are kept, does not cover a refinement. Whether a decision on old inputs actually changes there is not checked: a refined unknown is data, and substituting data leaves a type's sort and head as they were, so the common readings (`.prop`, `.other`, a sort) are kept; a Π-type value that captures the unknown reads its capture's reading through `telescope`, which can differ once the capture is a constructor value. Both point at main's assessment of decisions that read no table of unknowns. With the lead.

### D58. The table of unknowns never shrinks: swept, not proved (fifth lead, 2026-11-21; swept)

`access_absTy` (an access forgets no unknown) is true by inspection, and its proof is an induction over the whole interpreter, so by the lead's decision it stays a named obligation and the sweep checks it. Two checks were added. The ends probe (`lake exe sweep --ends`) now also runs, from every recorded state, an access of each variable of the top frame (`accessPath` then `accessInside`) and compares the table of unknowns and the three record lists before and after each access and each end. The main sweep compares consecutive recorded states of one run. Results on the suite: 358,141 accesses (85 failed or ran out of fuel) and 25,451 ends, none shrinking the table or a record list; no access grew the table (the 399 ends that do are all with typing on, D53); 68,120 consecutive pairs of recorded states, none shrinking it. On the probes: 1,469 accesses and 519 pairs, none.

### D59. Types mean codes closed under the type formers (the lead's A; fifth lead, 2026-11-21; built, see the end of this entry)

**Why.** A type's meaning is a carrier tagged by its level (`TyM`), and its structure lives beside it in the type the run returns (`TyS.shape`, a function type's parameters and result; `TyS.data`, a data type's name and parameters). Every clause that produces a type from a run has to copy that structure, and some do not, so the model is wrong on accepted programs: a call's result has no function shape (A11, `let a = Adder(n); a(Z)`); a parameter whose type a call computes has none (A18, `UseFn (f : Fn(Nat))`, and the predicate idiom); a field's type loses it (A12); a binding holding a type value needed a special convention (A19, `Value.strS`: the run of a type term carries the structure of the type it denotes in its result's TYPE); and an untyped proof leaves a constructor's parameter unsolved (A17, `BoxProof`). Separately, the elements of `Type₁` include `Prop` and `Type₀` themselves, which the level-2 decoding cannot read (`Apply1.lean`'s reported gap). D22's plan (a W-type `Str` beside `TyS`) is superseded.

**Lean's way** (docs/33 entry 3; Carneiro §6.4). A type means a code: a function type's code is `(Π, A, B)` with `B` a function from the elements of `A`'s decoding to codes; a universe `(U, n)` decodes to the set of codes of level `n`; a type's elements are taken from its code's decoding. The tags change none of the reasoning (Theorem 6.9).

**The code type.** Lean the proof assistant cannot define codes together with their decoding (induction-recursion). The usual encoding indexes each code by the Lean type it decodes to. There is one family per supported level, as `TyM` and `Sem` have one constructor per level now. The codes hold no `Sem`, so they are small, and a universe can decode to the codes of the level below:
- `Code0 : Type → Type 1`: `prop P` decoding to `PLift P`; `nat`; `unit`; `data n ps A unpk` (the declaration's name, its parameters as `Par0`, the carrier `A`, and the reading of an element as its constructor and its fields, each a coded element `Elt0`; since D61 the code holds no reading: `data d ps A`); `pi C t` (a telescope code decoding to `C`); `err` (decoding to `PEmpty`).
- `Tel0 : Type → Type → Type 1`, indexed by the final contents accumulated so far and by the carrier: `dat B c` decodes to `B × Fin`; `ref U c` decodes to `Back Fin U`; `param isRef A c C k` decodes to `(a : A) → C a`, where `k : (a : A) → Tel0 Fin' (C a)` gives the rest of the telescope at each ELEMENT of the parameter's carrier, as Lean's `B`. `Fin'` is `Fin × A` at a borrow parameter.
- `Elt0` (a coded element: a carrier, its code, an element) and `Par0` (a parameter: a type, as its code, or a coded value).
- `Code1 : Type 1 → Type 2` and `Tel1`: `lift` of a level-0 code (to `ULift`); `univ0` (`Prop`, decoding to `ULift Prop`: its elements are untagged propositions, as Lean's `T₀ = U₀`); `univ1` (`Type₀`, decoding to `(A : Type) × Code0 A`, the codes of level 0, as Lean's `⦇(U, 0)⦈ = T₀`); `pi`. `Code2`/`Tel2` likewise, with `univ2` decoding to `(A : Type 1) × Code1 A`, which holds `Prop` and `Type₀` themselves, so the level-2 gap closes.
- `TyM := t0 A (c : Code0 A) | t1 A (c : Code1 A) | t2 A (c : Code2 A)`; the level is read off the tag and the code (a proposition at 0, a universe at its successor). `Sem` is unchanged but for `ty (T : TyM)`, and is no longer mutual with `TyM` (D32's mutual block goes: the codes hold no `Sem`).
Viability checked before writing this (a scratch file, not in the build): Lean accepts the mutual families with their nested lists, the level-1 code of `Π(A : Type₀). Π(x : A). A`, a function-type code as an element of `Type₀`'s decoding, and application along a telescope by structural recursion through `k a`.

**Decoding.** The carrier is the index: the level-0 carrier of `t0 A c` is `A`, by definition. Encoding an element as a meaning and decoding a meaning recurse on the code: a datum is `Sem.dat` read through `unpk` (D32's self-describing value is unchanged), a proposition's element is `⋆`, a universe's element `⟨A, c⟩` is `.ty (t0 A c)`, anything else is read by its tag (`Sem.el0 A a`). Membership becomes one clause: a meaning is a member of a type when it is the encoding of an element of the type's carrier (Lean: elements are taken from the decoding); D32's data clause and the universes' level clause become its instances.

**Function types, closures and calls.** The `.pi` and `.fix` clauses build the telescope code by running each domain term, and the codomain, at the prefix of arguments encoded from carrier elements (`k a` runs the next term at `acc ++ [enc c a]`). A run that returns a type at another level, or no type, gives the `err` code, as `err` does now. A closure means an element of the code's carrier built along the code (`mkTele`, by recursion on the telescope code instead of a list of parameters). A call applies the callee along the code of its type (`applyTele`), and the result's type is the code the telescope reaches. So a call's result keeps its full structure (A11), and a parameter typed by a call (`Fn(Nat)`) is bound at the code the call computed (A18). Through a universe parameter the code is kept too: `id(Nat → Nat, f)` returns at the argument's own code. A Π into a proposition stays a proposition (`piProp`), computed from the domains' codes. `Apply1`'s level abstraction (`Lvl`) becomes the three instances over the families.

**What `TyS` becomes:** `{ m : TyM, label : Option Nat }`. `shape` and `data` are deleted; both are read off the code (`TyM.tel?`, `TyM.dataName?`), so no clause can drop them. Deleted with them: `PiParam`, `PiOut`, `piCar0`/`piCar1`/`piCar2`/`piCarL`, `CodeMeaning.tyShape`, `Value.tyShape`, `Value.strS`, `TyS.ofTyM`, the data model's `fieldData` and `paramData`, the shape plumbing in `psOf`, `mkBind` and the `.val` clause, the convention that a type term's run carries the denoted type's structure in its result's TYPE (the code is in the value), and the shape and data components of `bindSkel` and `StrAgree` (they become equality of codes). `K.Δ σ` and `K.Λ ℓ` keep their roles, now holding codes.

**The data model.** `car d ps` and its laws keep `Sem` parameters (the reference state holds meanings). `tyM d ps` decodes the parameters to `Par0` at the declared parameter types and returns the data code, whose `unpk` reads the fields as `Elt0` at their field codes. `fieldTy` returns codes, so a field of function type keeps its structure (A12). `DataGeneric` builds the same carriers.

**A17, beside it.** An erased place (a proof read where the checker reads a statement) returns `⋆` with the place's stored type instead of `err`, so a constructor parameter solved by a proof field's type is solved (`BoxProof`). A proof that is not a place keeps `err`.

**What the four core lemmas need.** Their statements are unchanged. The cases that change are those that build a type: `.pi`, `.fix`, `.val`, a call, a constructor's result type, and the parameter unification. Each is the same argument on a code instead of a shape. The telescope's continuation is a function of the arguments built from runs of subterms, so congruence and substitution follow from the induction hypothesis under `funext`, as `psOf`'s functions do now. `Apply1`'s laws (calling a built function runs its body; the round trips at each level; `closureAt_mem`) are re-proved by the same recursions on the telescope code. Codes are compared by equality, extensional through `funext`; nothing needs decidable equality.

**Order of work.**
1. `Code.lean`: the families, encoding, decoding, membership, the level, the round trips; sanity by `rfl` on the carriers of `id`, `Swap` and `UseFn`'s parameter.
2. `Apply.lean`/`Apply1.lean` on telescope codes.
3. The data model on codes.
4. `Value.mean`, `codeMeaning`, `Value.tyS` (no `tyShape`).
5. `Run.lean`'s clauses.
6. Re-prove the four core lemmas, then `AssignCongr`, `HintIndep`, `Mono`, `BotWrite`.
7. The step statements' type components (`bindSkel`, `StrAgree`).
8. The regress files: `ShapeAgree`'s negative lemmas become positive ones (a call's result has the callee's codomain code; the parameter's type is the code the call computed), and `UseAdder`, `UseFn`, `UseFnLet`, `UsePred` (to be written), `BoxProof` and `Alias` run in the model beside the checker's verdicts.
9. The sweeps and docs.
Larger than D32 (a few days), mostly mechanical. The risk is in step 5's `.fix` clause: a closure is a member of its own type only if the carrier it is built in is the index of the type's code exactly (`closureAt_mem`).

**One choice for the lead.** Universes decode to codes (above; Lean's §6.4), or to raw Lean types, which would let the codes hold `Sem` and stay in its mutual block, with a smaller change to the data model. With raw universes a type passed through a universe parameter loses its code inside the callee and in the result the telescope reaches, so `let g = id(Nat → Nat, f); g(Z)` fails in the model while the checker accepts it, and the level-2 gap stays. Recommended: codes.

**Built** (branch `codes`, merged into `spine`). `lake build OchrMeaning` is green, and the four core lemmas are re-proved on codes (`#print axioms`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`), as are every proof that stood before (no `sorry` was added; one went). Built as designed, with these differences:
- the Π and `fix` codomain has one definition, `Run.codOf` (a `PiCod`), and the body's hint `fixCod` is its plain type, so the body and a deferred parameter type read the same function;
- a call's argument is hinted at the next parameter of the callee's telescope (`TyM.paramAt?`), and the step statements read the callee's codomain off the code at the arguments (`TyM.codAt?`): `closeCall_step_stmt`'s `hkind`, `hres`, `hfills`, `htys` and `callType_step_stmt` are restated on the telescope;
- `FillShape` is one clause (the filler's run carries the loan's type); `StrAgree` is one equation of codes; `bindSkel` is (code, reading); `GlobalsOk.fn` ties a function's type code to its closure term's run; `piShape_step_stmt` compares the codes the original and the captured term run to;
- A17 is built beside it: an erased PLACE is `⋆` at the place's stored type (`SM.erasedAt`, `placeTy`), as the checker types a proof read at a place; a proof that is not a place stays untyped;
- (Ty) is deleted from `ShapeFacts` and from the sweep, with the chain it fed (`strS_updAt_bot`, the `htype` hypotheses, `copy_sort`): it was needed only because a binding's structure was read off the value it held;
- `DataModel.rank_lt` is restated on the element a recursive field packs (`Sem.elt`, what `semRank` ranks); as first stated it was about a tagged element `el0 (car d ps') y`, which no field has been since D32;
- the data half needs a condition the design did not name, `DataModel.CodeFaithful` (the code of `D(ps)` reads its elements back), for a packed element to be a member of its type (`ctor_mem_tyM`); for recursive and nested declarations see D61, which removed it.

Deleted: `PiParam`, `PiOut`, `piCarL` and the level carriers, `piShapeOf`, `tyShape`, `tyDataV` (and their congruences at two futures, two assignments and under substitution), `Value.strS`, `TyS.ofTyM`, `fixOut`, the data model's `fieldData` and `paramData`, `TyS.dataDecl` (dead since D32).

The concrete runs the lead asked for, kernel-checked on the standard axioms:
- first, before the bulk re-proof (`Regress/CodeClosures.lean`): a recursive function (`double(3) = 6`, a member of its type's code), a function returning a function (`Adder(2)` typed at `Π(m : Nat). Nat`'s code, then `5`), a function taking a function (`App(succ, 3) = 4`), and `id` at a function type, each built along its type's code as the `.fix` clause builds it;
- the motivating programs, run by the model's own `run` (`Regress/CodeRuns.lean`), each beside the checker's verdict (`ShapeProbes`, `AdequacyProbes`): `UseAdder` (`let a = Adder(2); a(3)` is `5`); `UseFn` (`Fn(Nat)` runs to `Π(x : Nat). Nat`'s code, a parameter typed `Fn(Nat)` is bound at it, `f(3)` applies `f`) and `UseFnLet`; `UsePred` (new probe, accepted, with a rejected control: `Pred(Nat)` runs to `Π(x : Nat). Prop`'s level-1 code through a level-2 closure, and `P(3)` is the proposition `P(3)`); `Alias`; `let g = id(Nat → Nat, f); g(Z)`; `BoxProof` (`MkPBox(h)` solves `A := True` off `h`'s stored type). The globals are given as the closures the `.fix` clause builds along their codes, with the bodies as functions on meanings; the runs of the bodies' own terms are not checked here.
- `Regress/ShapeAgree.lean`'s negative lemmas are positive: a call's result is typed at the code its callee's telescope reaches (`call_cod`), and the run of a Π-term returns the type value holding its code (`pi_code`).

Sweeps rerun (`CtxOk` lost (Ty), and `GlobalsOk.fn` is restated, which no sweep reads): on the suite's 70,207 recorded states and the 538 probe states every real clause holds (both planted clauses reported); the ends probe is as in D58 (25,451 ends and 358,141 accesses on the suite, none shrinking the table of unknowns or a record list, 399 ends growing it, all with typing on; 328 ends and 1,469 accesses on the probes, none).

### D60. A recursive closure's stages are filled (fifth lead; built, a finding)

Built strictly, as the `.fix` clause did, every recursive closure meant `err` (`Regress/CodeClosures.lean`, `Old.closure_err`, kernel-checked): stage `k` is defined only on arguments of rank below `k`, a carrier holds total functions only, so no stage's closure could be built, and every recursive call failed. A stage's closure is now built with the leaf filled where the stage is undefined (`mkTelFillL`, `closureFillAt`: a classical choice of an element of the leaf's type, where it is inhabited). The guarded fixpoint reads a stage only below its rank, so the filler is never observed by a call that decreases; `double(3) = 6` and the recursive closure is a member of its type's code (`double_member`).

### D61. A recursive declaration's code would hold itself; a nested one's cannot be built (fifth lead; the lead chose (a), before the call steps; built, see the end of this entry)

D59's data code is `data n ps A unpk`, whose `unpk` reads an element as its constructor and its fields, each a coded element at its field's code. Codes are an inductive family, so no code holds itself. For a recursive declaration, a field of type `D(ps')` must be read at `D(ps')`'s own code, which would hold the code being built. This can still be done by stages: an element of rank `k + 1` reads its recursive fields at the stage-`k` code, which agrees with the full one on elements of lower rank. For a NESTED declaration it cannot. `Rose(A) := Node(a : A, kids : List(Rose(A)))`: the field `kids` is read at `List(Rose(A))`'s code, whose parameter is `Rose(A)`'s code itself, because a type parameter's meaning is its code (`Par0.ty`). No stage's code is the full one there, and the full one would be a proper part of itself. (D32's `TyM.data` with an unpacking into meanings had the same cycle for nested declarations.)

What it touches. No proof built so far: `tyM` is defined from an abstract `DataModel`, and the four core lemmas and the step skeleton never build a recursive model. It touches the assembly. `DataModel.CodeFaithful` (a packed element is a member of its type) holds for a non-recursive declaration with small fields and parameters that are members of their types, but no data model can satisfy it for a nested one. `DataGeneric.lean` reads a constructed carrier at a stand-in code (`opq`, a data code naming no declaration). Its recursive and nested fields never matched the run's meaning, not since D32, so its `FieldsFaithful` is the type part only, and the `self`/`nest` case is recorded as open there.

Options:
- (a) A data code names its declaration, its parameters and its carrier, and nothing else. The encoding reads an element's constructor and fields through the data model (`Code0.enc`, decoding and membership take the model). Codes stay small and well-founded, and nested declarations are fine. The cost: membership and encoding take the model, which every run has (`K.dm`).
- (b) Keep `unpk` and code recursive declarations by stages. Nested declarations stay out, although the checker accepts them (`DataGeneric`'s `nest`).
- (c) Coinductive codes, which Lean does not have.

Recommended: (a). It is D32's "a data type means a code naming `d` and `ps`" without the unpacking, and it is a change to the data half only.

**The lead's decision: (a), before the call steps.** It is definitional (what a data code is), and everything about calls is stated on codes, so it should not change under them; a recursive type's code containing itself is the mistake Lean's model avoids (a W-type's code names its index set and family, never its own decoding: docs/papers/theory-of-lean.pdf §6.2.1, §6.4); and (b) would leave nested types, which the checker accepts, without a meaning.

**Built** (branch `datacodes`, merged into `spine`). `lake build OchrMeaning` is green; no `sorry` was added.
- *The code.* `Code0.data (d : IndDecl) (ps : List Par0) (A : Type)`: the declaration, its parameters' meanings, its carrier. `Unpk0` and `Elt0.sems` are deleted. A reading `DataRead` (`IndDecl → List Sem → (A : Type) → A → Nat × List Sem`) reads an element as its constructor and its fields' meanings; encoding, decoding and membership take it (`Code0.enc R`, `Code0.dec R`, `s ∈ₛ[R] T`), and so do the level instances (`Lvl.read`, `lvl0 R`), `piTyAt`, `closureAt`, `callOf`, `unifyTyS` and the step statements, always at the run's `K.dm.read`.
- *The data model carries its reading.* `DataModel.read`, by default `readOf car tag fields`, with the law `read_eq`: at a covered declaration it reads an element as the model packs it (`tag`, `fields`). A model given its own fields (every toy model, `libModel R hR`) meets the law by `simp`.
- *`CodeFaithful` disappears.* What a packed element needs to be a member of its type is now only that its parameters are read back from their codes (`ParamsCoded d ps`: each a type value or a member of its declared type) and the law `read_eq`; `ctor_mem_tyM` is a theorem of every data model, for every covered declaration whose parameters are coded: non-recursive, recursive (`List`) and nested (`Rose(A)`, with a `List(Rose(A))` field) alike, because the code reads no field. `pmem_partly` takes the same hypotheses.
- *The generic model* (`DataGeneric.lean`) encodes its leaves at a reading it is given (the class `Reading`) and owes that the reading reads each covered declaration's elements as it packs them (`Coherent Γ`, the argument `hR` of `genericModel`). It still reads a constructed field at the stand-in code `opq` (a data code naming no declaration), so its recursive and nested fields are not yet the run's meanings; D61 removes the reason they could not be.

The concrete checks, first, kernel-checked on the standard axioms, each beside the checker's verdict (`Regress/ShapeProbes.lean`, asserted by the build: `Len` accepted, `LenTwo` (`Len(Cons(3, Cons(4, Nil))) = 2` by `refl`) accepted and `LenThree` rejected; `Rose`, `Tree` (`Node(1, Cons(Node(2, Nil), Nil))`), `Root` and `RootOfTree` (the root is `1`) accepted, `RootOfTreeBad` rejected):
- `List(Nat)`, recursive (`Regress/DataCodes.lean`): every list's meaning is a member of `List(Nat)`'s code (`semL_mem`); `Cons(3, Cons(4, Nil))` built by `ctorM` (`build_list`) and matched by reading its constructor and fields off it (`match_list`); `Len`, built along its type's code by filled stages as the `.fix` clause builds it (D60), is a member of its type's code and gives `2` at `[3, 4]` (`len_member`). Its body is given as a function on meanings, as in D59's runs; the run of its `match` term is not checked.
- `Rose(Nat)`, nested: every tree's meaning is a member of `Rose(Nat)`'s code and every forest's of `List(Rose(Nat))`'s, whose parameter is `Rose(Nat)`'s code itself (`semR_mem`, `semLR_mem`); the tree built (`build_rose`) and matched (`match_rose`).
- The model's own `run` of the same terms (`Regress/CodeRuns.lean`): `Cons(3, Cons(4, Nil))` runs by the constructor clause to the list's meaning, which the match clause reads as `Cons` (`indTag`) with the tail at `List(Nat)`'s code (`Sem.stepField`) (`run_list34`); the probe's `Tree` runs to the tree's meaning, read as `Node` with its `kids` at `List(Rose(Nat))`'s code (`run_tree`); the parameter `Rose(Nat)` of `List(Rose(Nat))` runs to `Rose(Nat)`'s code (`runParams_listRose`).

The four core lemmas are re-proved (`#print axioms`: `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`), as are D59's runs and closure checks; the repair was mechanical (the reading passed where membership, encoding or a level instance is used). Deleted: `Unpk0`, `Elt0.sems`, `eltsOf`, `CodeFaithful`. Sweeps not rerun: `Good` and `CtxOk` changed only in that membership takes the run's reading (`pmem K.dm.read`); no syntactic clause changed, and the sweep evaluates no membership.

**The status of `exists_dataModel`.** Still `sorry`, as it was. It owed a data model covering the accepted program (the generic construction, with `rank_lt` not covering nested paths and the field types' data part open); it now also owes a reading coherent with that model (`Coherent`). The model's fields are full meanings, a data field's read through the reading, and the reading must read the model's elements as the model packs them: the model and its reading are a fixed point. Before D61 the same content was asked of the code (`CodeFaithful`), which no model could meet for a nested declaration; it is now asked of the reading, a function on elements, which is to be defined by recursion on an element's rank (its fields are of lower rank). Not attempted.

### D62. The call steps: the decomposition (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; statements built, assemblies next)

The lead's B: `closeCall`, `callFn`/`runBodyCore` against "apply the callee's meaning", and the re-run of a sealed program, decomposed into named obligations, the risk-bearing leaves first.

**The statements, restated in the simulation form** (`Steps/Statements.lean`). The old forms ran the reference from `SM' K ρ st` and concluded `Below`, which D20 found false once a borrow was held. Now:
- `callFn_step_stmt`: from the checker's state with the head and the arguments still counted as temporaries (`MState.withTemps`, as `evalCall` holds them before its pops) and any related reference state, with the reference's arguments the checker's read through the renaming (`ArgsAgree`) and the callee's reading `fres`, for a call that is neither a statement nor erased (`hns`): `SimConclT` against `callOf` (the result, its type, the relation and `Good` after).
- `runBody_step_stmt`: against the reference's run IN PLACE (`CallRef.runBodyRef`: the callee's frame pushed on the caller's state, each borrow parameter held at its argument's label, the body run in tail position at the codomain, the frame popped with its borrows ended but the returned one). The induction hypothesis reaches only a reference run from a state related to the checker's, and the checker runs the body in place; `callOf` runs it in the closure's own frame. So the comparison with `callOf` is a lemma about the reference alone.
- `closeCall_step_stmt`: EXACT against `callOf` (as before, with `hkind`, `hres`, `hfills`, `htys` on the reference's arguments).
- `nfSealed_step_stmt`: now equality (no borrow ends early since D29), under an assignment extending `ρ` at the unknowns the nested run created (D20 item 9); the environment, goal and records in force unchanged, the table of unknowns only grown, `Good` after. Hypotheses: the text's loans are live borrows of the state (its holes), its tail reads no hint ((WF16)), and its reference run succeeds.
`runBody_step_stmt` and `nfSealed_step_stmt` take "the reference run succeeds" (`hok`) because both run untyped code, and an untyped run of arbitrary text is not claimed (D20 item 4).

**The tree.** `evalCore`'s call (`evalCall`): the head (`eval`, in place for a place), the arguments (`eval` each at `argHint`; none is a statement, since the class is 0), the pops, then `callFn`:
1. the call's type (`callType`/`declCallType` against the code's `codAt?`), `recCheck`, the row (`declKind` against the codomain's being a borrow) and class (0, from `hns`), all decisions or alignments;
2. the lending (static ends, rule 1): the marks `lent_g` for a borrow result, which are no site of a live borrow;
3. a neutral callee: `closeCall` (exact); a known one: `runBody`, then `lendEnv (.ended g [k])`, against `CallRef.runCallRef`, then `callOf_inPlace`; a stuck body: `catchStuck` restores the state at the call (the monad's `tryCatch` on `StateT`), then `closeCall`;
4. η at `Unit` (`etaUnit res r`): the callee's meaning is a member of its type, so its result at a `Unit` codomain is `()`.
`runBody`: the frame pushed (captures, `self`, parameters at `paramTypes`' types or deferred ones) against `callFrame`; the body by the induction hypothesis (untyped, at `declCallType`'s hint against the code's codomain, not erased since the class is 0); `wholeReturned`, the pop (`dropTopBind` each binding, `endBorrow` of each borrow held) against `popCallFrame` (the order of the ends by `relend_comm`).
`closeCall`: the cells, the result and fills against `callOf` (`closeCall_means_data` and its `&T` twin), the substitution of each fill for its loan (`substEnv`, [End]'s substitution: `meanV_substLoan`, proved, and the re-runs it causes, `nfSealed_step`), `canonNeutral` (the path condition), `lendEnv (.ended ℓ …)` against `callOf`'s `setLent`.
`nfSealed`: the nested run (`nestedRun_sim`), `restoreKeep`, `lentTo` (a mark does not change a meaning), the stuck case (`canonNeutral` of the program itself).

**The risk-bearing leaves, ranked** (`Steps/Call.lean`, each `sorry` with what it rests on):
1. `nestedRun_sim`, the nested run of a re-run (D20 item 3, open since the second lead). Once the nested run binds or writes one of the program's holes (a fill's `*r := loan_k` does at once), its state holds a loan outside any sealed program whose borrow is not in its environment, which (WF2) forbids, and its typed futures say nothing at that label, so the state is not `Good` and the induction hypothesis does not apply. The fix is a relative invariant: the holes typed by the outer state, as `GoodF` types the labels in flight, which changes `Good` in every statement; the sweep must check the relative clauses on nested states before it becomes a clause. Every stuck call over a borrow depends on it, since every [End] of a fill's borrow re-runs the fills. I judge this the riskiest: it is a change of the invariant, not a lemma.
2. `callOf_inPlace` (the reference alone): the in-place run is the application of the closure's meaning. It rests on the frame property of `run` (a run reads and writes only the top frame and the sites of the labels it holds), the counter lemma (`run_counter_indep`, open), the cells (a borrow parameter's final content, read back through a cell after the ends, is what `endLabel` fixes in the caller at the pop), the lending bookkeeping (the pop's `relend` gives the returned borrow a loan site where its argument was lent; `callOf` and the checker's `.ended g [k]` give a fill site there, so `runCallRef` resets the sites as fills), and, for a recursive callee, the fixpoint equation under [Rec] (the closure applied at the arguments is the body's run with `self` the closure itself; needs every recursive call to decrease, `hacc`; its statement needs the `.fix` clause's stages named outside the clause, a mechanical refactor of `Run.lean`).
3. `closeCall_means_data` and its `&T` twin: [Close]'s sealed programs mean the reference's call. Attacked on one instance before (`Attack/CloseCall*.lean`, both halves proved); the general form is large and believed routine.
4. The untyped runs: a body and a re-run run untyped (`eval fuel' false`). KERNEL-CHECKED AND FIXED (D64): `evalCore_step_stmt` and `eval_step_stmt` were false of the untyped run of an unannotated `Nil`, and are now restricted to untyped runs whose reference run succeeds.
5. `run_counter_indep` (Lemmas, open, believed true): the reference runs a closure's code and a sealed program from the counter 0, the checker at its global counter.

**The bookkeeping leaves** (statements with the assemblies): `callFrame` and `popCallFrame` against the checker's push and pop; the call's type and the hints (`argHint` against `domAfter`, `declCallType` against the codomain, `paramTypes` against the binders); the class 0 from `hns`; the marks and `.ended g [k]`; `catchStuck`; η at `Unit` (the callee's membership); `lentTo`; `canonNeutral_step` (existing).

**Progress on the concrete calls (item 3; `Regress/CallInPlace.lean`).** Done, kernel-checked: `UseInc` and `UseId1` by `callOf` give the checker's answers; `Inc(&y)` by `callOf` and in place agree. Found: a place a returned borrow is lent at reads its final content raw in the checker and decoded in `callOf` (D66, for the lead: `Rel` should compare at typed futures).

**Order.** The nested-run invariant first (it changes `Good`, so the earlier it is settled the less is re-proved), with the sweep of its clauses on nested states; then the kernel check of the untyped `Nil`; then `callOf_inPlace`'s frame property and the cells on a concrete call with a borrow parameter and one returning a reborrow (the reference's own run, kernel-checked, beside the checker's verdicts), then its proof; then the assemblies; then [Close]'s meanings.

### D63. Where the checker runs a sealed program that has a hole (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; for main's D97)

The lead holds D62's relative invariant until main has assessed D97 (a sealed program with a live hole is never run; it runs when its last hole is filled). `nestedRun_sim` is restated for a program with no loan, under plain `Good`, which both outcomes need. For the D97 lane, every place the checker runs a sealed program's text from an empty environment (line numbers of `Machine.lean` at spine ecbcd055, main eed45c43), and when that text can hold a hole (the loan of a live borrow, or of one about to be live):

1. **`nfSealed`, through `substV`'s sealed case (809)**, wherever a substitution changes a sealed program, at every position `mapState` reaches (`substEnv`, 851). A program's loans are its holes: since every [End] substitutes everywhere, a `Good` state holds no loan of a dead borrow, so any loan left in a re-run text is a live borrow's or a new one's. The substitutions:
   - `endBorrow` (933), `substEnv (.loan ℓ) c`: every program over `ℓ` re-runs with the final content `c` in place of the hole. When `c` holds a live loan (a reborrow of `*ℓ` that outlives it; the same `c` `lendEnv (.ended ℓ (liveLoansIn c))` reads next), the re-run has that loan as a hole. A program with a second hole besides `ℓ` keeps it too. (The lead's second case.)
   - `closeCall`'s `&T` row (2962), `substEnv (.loan ℓ) ⌈…; *r := loan_k; peek cⱼ⌉`: a program over `ℓ` (an earlier stuck call's fill, when `ℓ` is itself a borrow an earlier stuck call returned) re-runs with this fill's text inside, whose `loan_k` is the borrow the call returns. `k` is not yet in Ω at the re-run (`borrow_k` is returned after), so `nfSealed`'s `holes` (live loans only) misses it, and it is live as soon as the call returns.
