A rule that tests "a live hole" at the re-run does not catch this case; it needs "a loan of a borrow not yet ended", or the substitution done after the result is placed. (Not in the lead's count.) The data row (2973) substitutes a fill with no hole, so its re-runs have only the holes the program already had.
   - `refine` (2987), `substEnv (.abs σ) r`: every program mentioning `σ` re-runs, fills over live borrows included. (The lead's first case is the next item; this one is beside it.)
   - `refine`'s `renormAll` (2989, 3087; `renormV` 3096 calls `nfSealed` on every sealed program after re-normalising its text), when `σ` is a generalisation's: every sealed program of the state re-runs, every fill over a live borrow included. (The lead's first case.)
   - the neutral substitutions, `substEnv n σ`/`substEnv n r` for a generalised or recorded sealed program `n`: `generalizeNeutral` (3044), `generalizeFound` (4067), `evalMatch` (3015) and `evalMatchInd` (3336) on a recorded neutral, `etaPlace` (3392), `movedPlace` (3452). A program that contains `n` as a subterm re-runs (a program equal to `n` is replaced, not run), a fill over a live borrow included.
   Not sources of holes: `substV` on a goal (`rewriteGoal`, 1984, 1987) or an expected type (3016, 3019, 3173, 3327, 3337, 3341), since types and the goal hold no loan ((WF11)); `lendEnv`'s `mapState` (454) changes marks only and runs nothing.
2. **`sealedType?` (1482)**, through `valType?` of a sealed value (1461) and `funType?` of a sealed callee (2659, D56): the text typed (`eval fuel' true`) on a private copy from an empty environment. A fill sits at a lent place, which a read ends first, so a fill with a live hole should not reach `valType?` on an accepted run; not checked.

So the count is: the lead's two (`renormAll` after `refine`; `endBorrow` when the content holds a live loan), and beside them `refine`'s own substitution, the neutral substitutions, `closeCall`'s `&T` row with a hole not yet live, and `sealedType?` to be confirmed unreachable with a hole.

**Under D97 (main 2222d517; spine rebased onto 03740f8d).** The rule is on the text: a sealed program whose text holds ANY loan is not run (`nfSealed` returns `⌈t⌉` as it is, "[Seal] left sealed"); it runs when its text holds no loan, i.e. at the substitution of its last hole. What each site above now does:
1. Every substitution (`substV`'s sealed case through `nfSealed`) substitutes into the text and runs it only if the result holds no loan:
   - `endBorrow`'s `substEnv (.loan ℓ) c`: a program over `ℓ` alone, with `c` loan-free, runs (hole-free); one with a second hole, or whose `c` holds a live loan (a reborrow outliving `ℓ`), stays sealed with `c` in its text.
   - `closeCall`'s `&T` row: the substituted text holds `loan_k`, so it stays sealed. The case a "live hole" test missed (`k` not yet in Ω) is caught, the rule being on any loan.
   - `refine`'s `substEnv (.abs σ) r`, `renormAll`/`renormV`, and the neutral substitutions (`generalizeNeutral`, `generalizeFound`, `evalMatch`, `evalMatchInd`, `etaPlace`, `movedPlace`): a fill over a live borrow stays sealed, its text substituted; only loan-free programs re-run. The old re-run fill handling (`liveLoansIn`/`lentTo`, D90's static ends) is gone from the checker.
2. `sealedType?`: it does meet holed fills on accepted runs ([Repack] at a whole-again point, in HashMap.Get, MapGetMut, DepVec.VGet), so the D63 conjecture that it is unreachable with a hole was false; under D97 it no longer runs such a text and reads the type off it as [Close] wrote it (a cell's declared type or initial value's type, a borrow-returning call's declared codomain content, a result form's head's codomain), a syntactic reading.

So every nested run is hole-free, and `nestedRun_sim` for a program with no loan under plain `Good` (`Steps/Call.lean`, `hfree : t.loans = []`) is the final form; `Good` needs no relative version (the lead's D62 invariant is dropped). `nfSealed_step_stmt` keeps its statement: a text with a loan is returned as it is (its meaning is itself, `ρ' = ρ`), a text without one is `nestedRun_sim`.

**The reference's fill handling already matches.** The reference never runs a text: a sealed program means its text run from the empty state at the future (`codeMeaning.sealed`, `meanV`'s `.sealed` clause), its holes read from the future, and [End] fixes the ended label in the future (`semEnd`), which every meaning, a fill's included, reads. There was no re-run of a fill on the reference's side before D97 either; what D97 removes is the checker's, and with it the obligation that a holed re-run mean the sealed program (the nested state holding loans whose borrows are outside it). What remains on the relation's side is the substitution lemma: a text with `loan ℓ := c` substituted means the text at the future that gives `ℓ` the meaning of `c` (`RunSubst.lean`, step (A)), which is what a substitution kept sealed needs. Fill sites are unchanged: a sealed program is a fill over every loan in its text (`Value.sitesOf`), and the substituted text holds exactly the loans of `c` and the other holes, which is where `relend` puts the reborrows' fills.

### D64. An untyped run of an unannotated constructor has no reference run (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; kernel-checked, statements restricted)

D20 item 4 said, read off the clauses, that `evalCore_step_stmt` and `eval_step_stmt` are false of untyped runs of arbitrary text. D59, D61 and D89 changed both sides since, so the lead asked for a kernel check before restricting. `Regress/UntypedNil.lean` (built, standard axioms): from `a : Nat ↦ 0` with `List` declared (`Good` at every assignment, `good_SL`), the checker's untyped run of `Nil` with no parameter written and no hint succeeds, through `evalCore` and through `eval` (the kernel evaluates both), and returns the bare constructor value with no parameter recorded and no type; the reference's `.ctor` clause solves `List`'s parameter from neither the fields (none) nor the hint, and fails (`run_nil_none`). So both statements as they stood are false at every context that admits the state and reads `Nil` as neither a statement nor erased (`evalCore_untyped_false`, `eval_untyped_false`, on copies of the old forms, as the earlier refutation records are).

Fixed: both take `hok : typed = false → (run K ρ φ (hintM K ρ hint) t s).isSome`, an untyped run claimed only of code whose reference run succeeds; the run above is excluded (`hok_excludes`). Accepted code meets it: its typed check rejects a constructor whose parameters nothing determines, and the untyped run reads the same hints (D88, D89); for a body, the callee's meaning being a member of its type gives it. The call statements had it already (D62). Other statements that take `typed` and conclude that the run succeeds (the matches, `letIn`, `evalCtor`, `eq`, `J`) are not restricted here: each gets the same hypothesis with its own counterexample, or where its assembly needs it.

### D65. The decision core after main's D96 (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; built)

Main's D96 (72fae0a9): each unknown's declared reading is frozen when `freshAbs` makes it, in a write-once table (`MState.absDecl`); the six decision readers consult it (`absReading`), `Decide.lean` no longer reads the unknowns' types, and a closure's or Π-type's captures are read by their recorded readings (`capReadings`). Spine is rebased onto it (ed4676eb).

The model now carries the frozen table as it carried the types. `DecideScope.EnvRel` and `DecCore` relate `absDecl` instead of `absTy` (`sim_absReading`, `sim_capReadings`; `decideSim` re-proved with no other change); `DecExtend.Grows` is stated on `absDecl`; `freshAbs_sim` and `runs_freshAbs_inv` push the reading with the type, and `runs_freshAbsR_inv` reads the reading off `valTypeDecl`, whose run leaves the state as it was (`runs_valTypeDecl_keep`, open: an induction over `Decide.lean`'s mutual block, the same fact as `copyRead_aligned`'s "state unchanged"). The static-lend probe states (`Regress/StaticLend.lean`) carry their unknowns' frozen readings.

What remains of `Steps/DecExtend.lean`:
- A refinement rewrites the unknowns' types and not their readings, so it keeps the decision core whole (`DecCore`: Σ, the readings, the reading mode, the top frame's readings): every decision on the same input gives the same answer before and after (`decideSim`), and `CtxOk.dec` transfers across it with nothing to prove beyond that the step keeps those four.
- A step that only adds unknowns extends the readings and rewrites none of them (`freshAbs` pushes, `restoreKeep` and `exitWorld` keep the table whole). An old input's answer never changes: `decAligned_grow`, stated, open, now only the simulation of `Decide.lean` between tables that agree below the old size, carrying that every unknown a decision reads is one its input or Σ names. A new input naming a new unknown reads the new reading, which is where the lead's (ii) extends the context's decisions.
- `runT_congrD`/`meanV_congrD` (the run's congruence in the decisions), unchanged and open.
- Left on main's side, per the lead: a refinement can still substitute into a sealed program embedded in a term (D55's preservation, now for sealed programs only), and the `liveScope` flag.

### D66. A place a returned borrow is lent at: the checker reads its final content raw, `callOf` reads it decoded (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; kernel-checked, for the lead)

Found by the concrete calls of D62's item 3 (`Regress/CallInPlace.lean`, `Regress/ReturnedRead.lean`, standard axioms), before any proof of `callOf_inPlace`.

**The concrete runs.** The reference's own runs of the checker's probes, by `callOf` (the callee's closure applied), give the checker's answers: `let y = 2; Inc(&y); y` runs to `3` (`useInc_callOf`) and `let y = 2; let r = Id1(&y); *r := 5; y` to `5` (`useId1_callOf`), with `Inc (x : &Nat) : Unit := *x := S(*x)` and `Id1 (x : &Nat) : &Nat := &*x` (`Regress/ShapeProbes.lean`: `UseIncIs3`, `UseId1Is5` accepted, `UseIncIs2`, `UseId1Is2` rejected). For `Inc` the call in place (`CallRef.runCallRef`) is computed too and agrees with `callOf`: the same result and, at every position, the same meaning, with no label held (`inc_agree`).

**The finding.** After `Id1(&a)`, run directly, the checker holds `a ↦ lent_k loan_k` (its real run at a hole, `ReturnedRead.Id1At`, checked by `#guard`), which means the future at `k`, whatever it is (`mark_loan_raw`). `callOf`'s `.ref` row leaves `a` reading the future at `k` DECODED at the result's code and passed through the backward function (`callOf_id1`): at a future whose value at `k` is not a member of `Nat` (`moved`), `a` reads `err` where the checker's `a` reads `moved` (`callOf_reads_decoded`); at a future whose value at `k` is a number, the two agree (`callOf_reads_typed`). A fill made by [Close] decodes too (the inner call's backward function reads the content written through `*r := loan_k`), so only a call that RUNS its body leaves the raw reading.

**Why it matters.** `Rel` compares meanings at every future that agrees with the run's off the reference's live labels (`RelF`), and the returned label `k` is live, so its value there is arbitrary. So after a borrow-returning call that runs its body, the checker's state and `callOf`'s are not related by `Rel` (they differ at `a` wherever the future gives `k` a non-member), and `callFn_step_stmt` is false as stated for such calls; `callOf_inPlace` holds only up to such futures. The theorem is not affected in spirit: every read of a state is at a future that fixes each live label to its holder's content (the closed future), and a borrow's content is a member of its type when it ends (D53 (h), D64's [Repack]); the program above reads `5` on both sides.

**Fix, for the lead.** (a) `Rel` (its `SimEq`) compares at the futures that also give each live label a member of its holder's content type (`RelF` and typed, as `TypedFutureF` types the labels in flight); at those futures decoding is the identity, and `callOf_inPlace` and `callFn_step_stmt` can hold. It changes `Rel` in every statement, and its lemmas (`Rel.start`, `sim_seq`, `Rel.private_copy`, `botWrite_rel`, `rel_refs`, `rel_neutrals`) must be re-proved; the reference state's holders carry their content types (`SBinding.ty`), so the typing is expressible on the reference side. (b) Make `callOf` read raw: impossible in general, since a backward function is a function of the decoded content. Recommended: (a).

**The lead's decision on D66: (a)**, `Rel` compares at the futures that give each live label a whole member of its holder's content type (a borrow's final content is one, D53), after three checks:

1. **`Id1(&y)` in place against `callOf`: they agree at typed futures** (`CallInPlace.id1_inPlace_typed`, kernel-checked): the same result (`2` at the reborrow), positions, holders and lending of the reborrow (a fill at `y`), and at every future giving the reborrow a number the same meaning at every position. The checker's state after `let y = 2; let r = Id1(&y)` is `y ↦ lent_k loan_k`, `r ↦ borrow_k 2` (`ReturnedRead`, `#guard` on its real run at a hole); position by position it reads as the reference's after `callOf` and the binding of `r` at typed futures (`y`: the future at `k`, which `callOf` decodes to itself at a number, `callOf_reads_typed`; `r`: `2`; `r` holds the reborrow on both sides; the reborrow is lent at `y` as a fill on both sides).
2. **Non-vacuity.** At a typed future the comparison still separates readings: a lender reading the reborrow's final content and one reading `7` differ at the future giving the reborrow `3`. The closed future: in `let r = &p; let z = (*r).0; let w = q; (*r).0 := z; w` (`ObsReturned.PartMove`, accepted) the read of `q` happens while `r`'s content is partly moved, so the closed future, which fixes `r`'s label to that content, is not typed. The read is still determined: `q` holds no loan of `r`'s label (it is not lent to it), so its reading does not depend on the future there (`run_congr`), and it equals its reading at the typed future that agrees elsewhere and gives the label the whole pair the field was moved out of (that pair is the witness that a typed future exists). One wrinkle for the restatement: a label held by a TEMPORARY has no content type in the reference state (`SFrame.temps` carry meanings only), so the typing of a live label must be read where it is lent (the stored type of each site's place at the site's path) or carried as a typed flight beside the state, as `GoodF` does on the checker's side.
3. **The `Id` observation.** `let r = IdL(&y); Id(Unit, side, side)` with both sides moving `*r` out (`ObsAfterCall`) is accepted, beside the rejected control `ObsAfterCallCtl` (one side does not move it); moving through the returned borrow inside a side and ending it there is rejected (`ObsInSide`, [D53]). A side that only reads through `r` leaves the lender out of the footprint (D68), so neither model observes it. A side that also borrows through `r` (`let w = &*r; let z = *r; ()`, `ObsBorrowThrough`) is accepted and puts the lender in the footprint: the checker's observation reads `y`'s raw value `lent_k loan_k`, which at the observation's closed future means the moved content, where the reference's `callOf` reads it decoded, `err`. The equation still holds in both (both sides read alike, and type-states make both sides move through the same borrow), so no false proposition is proved; but the observation's reads differ, which (a) does not cover, since the observation reads at a closed future that is not typed. Smallest fix, in my view: the checker rejects an observation whose footprint holds a place lent to a returned borrow whose content a side left moved (the [Id-typestate] family, as D53 rejects the end itself outside an observation); the alternative, the reference's observation reading a moved final content as moved at the lent place, has no general form, since a backward function is defined on whole contents only. Not fixed here; fixed by main's D98 (below).

**The lead's decisions after the checks (2026-11-20).** (1) A live label is typed by its HOLDER, where its content lives, not by its sites (a fill site sits at the lender, whose type is not the content's when a callee returns a borrow of a part: `Fst (p : &(Nat × Nat)) : &Nat := &(*p).0`). Built: the reference state records each borrow's content type when the borrow is made (`SState.heldTy`, set by `borrowPlace` and by `callOf`'s `.ref` row at the result's code; a binding's stored type gives the same), so every holder, a parked temporary included, has a type; the core lemmas and the regress records are carried through it (no `sorry` added). To check before the bulk re-proof: `Fst` (`let p = (2, 3); let r = Fst(&p)`), the checker's state beside the reference's, related at typed futures with the label typed `Nat` by its holder while its fill sits at `p`. (2) The observation gap goes to the checker lane as the uniform rule (a borrow never ends over a moved or partly moved content, in an observation's final ends as anywhere), being assessed on main; the observation step is to be stated with that as a named hypothesis, discharged by the rule or else a recorded gap with `ObsBorrowThrough` as its witness. Then `Rel` at typed futures and its lemmas.

**Main's D98 (2026-11-20) settles (2).** The checker's exception is removed: an observation's final ends are [End]'s, with its wholeness check, so `ObsR`, `ObsAfterCall`, `ObsAfterBorrow` and `ObsBorrowThrough` are now rejected (`Regress/ObsReturned.lean`, verdicts asserted by the build; `ObsAfterCallCtl` and `ObsInSide` were rejected before). The observation step needs no hypothesis of its own; the spine is rebased onto it (`RunLib.endBorrow_succ` follows the checker's new body).

### D67. Dependence within sites: stated; fails after `callOf`'s borrow row (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; kernel-checked, for the lead)

**Status after D68 (2026-11-20): a fact about the model that the simulation no longer needs.** Since a place is read at the future built from the records (D68), no proof transports a reading from one future to another, so the simulation does not use this invariant. What is built stays (`DepWithin.lean` with its two proved `callOf` rows; the `*` erasure in `pathKey`; the new `relend`, which re-roots every site at the holder; `KeyBridge.lean`), and the invariant may be finished later or never. Reads and writes by key and `Hole` are not started. One known oddity of the model's field step is recorded rather than fixed: on the partly moved number `S(⊥…)` the constructor field step `S.0` reaches the predecessor under key `(1, 0)` where the number step reaches it under `(0, 0)` (`Regress/StepKinds.lean`, `dws_fails_succField`); no place produces that path (the checker's walk reaches no number by a constructor field), so it matters to nothing but this invariant.

The lead's (A): the reference-only invariant that a place's reading depends only on the borrows lent at, over or under it, the static form of the old `access_FT` (after an access the place read is lent to no live borrow, so its reading does not depend on any live borrow's final content); restricting `Rel` to typed futures removed its old derivation from the relation at every future. Stated (`CallInPlace.DepWithinSites`): two futures that differ only at live labels with no site at the position on a path comparable with the one read (`dataHit`) give the same reading of the position at that path (`Sem.follow`), typed or not.

**It fails after `callOf`'s borrow row** (`CallInPlace.depWithinSites_fails`, standard axioms; replaced by `consumed_holder_constant` once (i') was built), in the reference's state after `let y = 2; let r = Id1(&y)` (`bindR`): the argument's consumed holder, temporary `0`, reads `moved` where the reborrow's final content decodes and `err` where it does not, so it depends on the reborrow, which has no site there. `callOf`'s `.ref` row fixes EVERY position through the decoding (`s.mapVals`, `| none => .err`), not only the lent places, so after a borrow-returning call every unrelated position depends on the result's label. Two fixes: (i) the model: the undecodable branch keeps the position's meaning (`| none => v φ'`), so a position that reads no argument label is unchanged and only the lent places depend on the new label, where its sites are; (ii) state the invariant only at futures typed at the labels that differ (there the decoding succeeds and an unrelated position is constant). Recommended: (i); it is one branch of `callOf`, with the core lemmas' `callOf` cases and the parking lemmas (`park_fix_inv`, `park_fix_ordKeep`, whose hypothesis names the `err` branch) to carry.

**Built: (i'), not (i).** The lead first approved (i) (`| none => v φ'`), but a proved lemma relied on the stronger form: `Locality.callOfL_ref_inv` (and its `RunOrd` twin) concludes the run invariants after a borrow-returning call for ANY label set, which needs that no position reads a consumed argument label after the call; under (i) a lent place still reads its dead argument label at an undecodable future, so those labels would have had to be in the set, a new hypothesis through the core induction. (i') keeps every proved statement: the undecodable branch fixes the consumed argument labels to `err` and leaves the rest of the position as it was (`| none => v (φ'.updList (fixes (refLabels.map fun _ => .err)))`). A position that reads no argument label is then unchanged in both branches (no dependence on the new label without a site; `CallInPlace.consumed_holder_constant`: the failing position of D67 reads `moved` at every future), and no position reads a consumed argument label after the call. Repaired: `park_fix_inv` and `park_fix_ordKeep` take the error fixing as an explicit list with its cover (`ee`, `hcovE`), their callers supply it, one `MonoHolders` case, the attack's states (`S₃`…: the consumed temporary is `moved` in both branches) and the concrete call records. (i') does not remove D66: at an untyped future the lent place still reads differently from the checker (the checker's mark reads the future raw; `callOf` cannot apply a backward function to a non-member), so the relation at typed futures stays.

**Step 2: the concrete states** (`Regress/DepSites.lean`, kernel-checked, standard axioms). The invariant holds on the five states the lead named, each the reference's state at that point (by its own run where it is computed, else written as `callOf`'s `.ref` row (i') and [End] give it, with the checker's sites where a checker state is at hand): the plain borrow (`Attack.S₂`, `borrowPlace`'s output); `Id1` after `let y = 2; let r = Id1(&y)` and after `*r := 5` (`CallInPlace.SR`); `Pick` at `Z` (sites: the checker's at `PZAt`); `G8` after the re-rooting end of `r`'s borrow, at a known `n` (sites: the reference's `relend` on the checker's, which `StaticLend` checks equal to the checker's after its `endBorrow`) and at an unknown `n` (sites tied to the same by evaluation, since the checker's sealed fills there call `Pick`); and D27's conditional-dependence state after `k`'s end (`x` reads `(back (ψ j, ψ m)).1`, which depends on `m` only when `j` holds `0`). All five hold for one reason: every position reads only labels lent at its root (a fill at a lent place, or a plain loan of a whole binding), and every path at a position is comparable with its root (`dws_of_root`). None of them has a site below a root.

**It fails at the first site below a root** (`dws_fails_SRb`). After `let r = σ1(&c1); let s = &*r` (the attack's program, by the reference's own run: `Attack.callOf_S₂`, `pushBindShaped_S₃`, `borrowPlace_deref_S₄`), `r` reads `s`'s final content and `s` is lent at `(r, [*])`. Read `r` at `[.0]`, a path that skips the `*` (ill-typed, since `r` is a borrow). By field keys `[.0]` and `[*]` are incomparable, so `s`'s label may differ between the two futures; yet the reading is the predecessor of `s`'s final content, because `*` is the identity on meanings (`Sem.step .deref`). Two typed futures giving the label `1` and `2` read `0` and `1`. The checker's `G8At` holds the same kind of site (`4` lent at `(r, [*])`), so the stated form fails there by the same argument (not separately checked). At every path that puts its `*`s where the type has borrows, the comparison is right; the failure is in comparing paths that place their `*`s differently.

**Fix, for the lead.** A meaning reads a path as it reads the path with its `*`s removed, so the comparison the invariant needs is by field keys with `*` erased (`DepWithinSitesE`, `dataHitE`). That form is a weakening of the stated one (`dwsE_of_dws`: it holds wherever the stated one does, the five states included), and the reborrow's state satisfies it (`dwsE_SRb`). Where to put the erasure:
- (a) In the model: the reference's access compares sites with `*` erased (`dataHit`, `tagHit`; or `pathKey` itself, which `lentPrefix` also reads). One comparison throughout the reference, and step 4's consumers take the invariant's hypothesis straight from the access. A probe (reverted): erasing in `dataHit`/`tagHit` alone breaks one lemma family (`AccessAbsorb.reached_absorb`, whose invariant `AbsorbInv` is stated on `pathKey`); erasing in `pathKey` breaks `AccessAbsorb` (the `*` at the head of a reborrowed site's path in `AbsorbInv`'s [End] step, `lentPrefix`'s index lemma) and `SitesWrite` (`pathKey_append` through `map`, a length fact). The modules built on those (`SitesFollow`, `UpdBot`, `DropOne`) were not reached by the probe. The agreement of the reference's access with the checker's (open) then needs that a place's path and a site's path at one position, both walks of the checker's value there, place their `*`s alike up to where they part.
- (b) In the invariant only: state it with `dataHitE`, the model unchanged, no proof touched. Each consumer bridges from the access's comparison to the erased one with the same walk lemma as (a)'s agreement, at each use.

The walk lemma is needed either way; (a) needs it once, at the access agreement, and makes the reference compare as its meanings read; (b) is the shortcut, with two comparisons of paths in the reference and a bridge between them at every consumer. Recommended: (a), erasing in `pathKey`, so that every comparison of paths in the reference (the access's, `lentPrefix`'s, the invariant's) is the one its meanings support; erasing in `dataHit` and `tagHit` alone would leave `lentPrefix` comparing positionally, a second comparison again.

**The lead's decision: (a)**, `*` erased in the model's path comparison (`pathKey`): a borrow means its content and `*` is the identity on meanings, so a comparison that distinguishes `*` steps compares something meanings do not have. Order: `pathKey` erased; `AccessAbsorb`, `SitesWrite` and what sits behind them repaired, reporting before any proved statement changes; the walk lemma once, at the access agreement; then the concrete states again.

**Applying it: two statements become false** (`Regress/StarErasure.lean`, kernel-checked, standard axioms, written with the erased key spelled out so they hold whatever `pathKey` is). Proof-only repairs went through (`pathKey_append`; `lentPrefix_above`, through a new `exists_take_key`: every prefix of a prefix's erased key is the key of a shorter prefix; `SitesWrite`'s key rewrites). Two proved statements depend on the positional comparison:
- `AccessAbsorb.AbsorbInv.endLabel` (the absorption invariant survives an end), on the reference's side (`absorb_endLabel_fails`). When the ended borrow's holder `r` is lent at `[]` as well as at `[*]`, the two sites have one erased key; [End] (`relend`) re-roots only the one under the `*`, so afterwards the site at `[]` has no fill at its key. Positionally the keys differ and the invariant could not hold before the end.
- `SitesWrite.sitesOf_updAt_bot` (the write of `⊥` removes exactly the sites at or under the written path), on the checker's side (`updAt_bot_fails`). At a mark directly over a borrow, `gloan 0 (borrow 1 Z)`, the write at `[*]` keeps the mark's site at `[]`, whose erased key is the written path's. Its helpers `not_under_of_key_ne` (a `*` against a field) and `not_under_strict` (a tail of `*`s only) are false as stated for the same reason.
Both counterexamples are states the checker never reaches: a site at a borrow holder outside its `*`, and a mark directly over a borrow, are both a borrow-typed place lent whole, which the checker rejects ("a borrow of a borrow-typed place"). Not yet reached by the build: `SitesFollow` (its key helpers `pathKey_cons_prefix` and `pathKey_prefix_cons` are positional by construction; `loanSite_blocks`, `site_not_bot` and `site_of_fillHole` look true under erasure, with new proofs), the checker clauses `WF12` and `WF14` (defined with `pathKey`, so erasure changes what the sweep checks), and the modules behind them.

**Proposed, for the lead.** (1) For `AbsorbInv.endLabel`: `relend` re-roots EVERY site at the ended borrow's holder, not only those under its `*` (the path below the holder, less a leading `*`). Under erasure this is the uniform form (the holder's meaning is the borrow's content, so a site anywhere at the holder lies in it), it is what step 3's [End] case of the invariant needs (a dependence through the holder at a site outside the `*` would otherwise stay behind), and at the checker's states it changes nothing (every site at a borrow holder is under its `*`), so `StaticLend`'s checks against the checker's `endBorrow` stand. Cost: `relendOne`, `RelendComm`, `mem_relend`'s statement. The alternative, a third clause of `AbsorbInv` established from `Rel` at `lentPrefix_absorbInv`, covers binding positions only through the holders clause. (2) For the checker's side: the checker walks values positionally (`stepV` tells `*` from a field), so statements about its own values (`SitesWrite`, `SitesFollow`, `WF12`, `WF14`) keep the positional key (today's `pathKey`, renamed), and the reference compares with the erased one. Where the two meet, positional to erased is free (a positional prefix is an erased prefix); the other direction is the walk lemma, which needs exactly the value form the checker guarantees (no mark directly over a borrow). The alternative, erasing there too, puts that hypothesis on each checker-side lemma and changes what the sweep checks.

**The lead's decisions (2026-11-20): both proposals.** (1) `relend` re-roots every site at the ended borrow's holder, with its path below the holder less a leading `*` (re-rooting only those under the `*` was a positional accident). (2) Two keys, each native to its side: the checker's statements about its own values keep the positional key, renamed `walkKey`; the reference compares with the erased `pathKey`; they meet in the relation, one way for free and the other way through the walk lemma, whose fact is to be a read-off clause and swept first. The lead's earlier "one comparison everywhere" holds in the MODEL; statements about the checker's own values tell `*` from a field because its values do.

**Built.**
- `SState.pathKey` drops `*` (`filterMap Step.key`); `SState.walkKey` is the old positional key. `SState.relend` (and `RelendComm.relendOne`) re-roots every site at the holder, at `inContent` of its path (the path less a leading `*`). `mem_relend` is restated for it (a site off the holder kept, a site at it adjusted); `relend_comm` keeps its statement. `AbsorbInv.endLabel` holds again, with a shorter proof (a site at the holder can no longer be left behind); `lentPrefix_above` is re-proved through `exists_take_key`. `lentPrefix` is unchanged: it matches sites by erased key, so it may find a prefix that differs from the place by `*`s only; its two uses take that case apart (`lentPrefix_absorbed`: accessing it whole is accessing the place; `lentPrefix_none_of_noop`: a site at the moved place itself contradicts `site_not_bot`).
- `SitesWrite`, `SitesFollow`, (WF12), (WF14) and `BotWriteGood`'s statements about values compare by `walkKey`, their proofs unchanged.
- `KeyBridge.lean`, where the keys meet. The free direction: a positional prefix or equality is an erased one (`pathKey_prefix_of_walkKey`, `pathKey_eq_of_walkKey`). The walk lemma: under (WF1), every site path at a position is fields only, or the borrow's `*` then fields (`site_shaped`), and every successful walk is fields only, or nothing, or the `*` then fields (`walk_shaped`); for such paths the erased comparisons give the positional ones (`walkKey_strict_of_site_walk`, `walkKey_prefix_of_sites`, `walkKey_prefix_of_walk_site`, `walkKey_take_of_site`, the last moving from `[]` to `[*]` where a site is the borrow's whole content). Used at the meeting points: `lentPrefix_fill` (`loanSite_blocks`, (WF12)), `lentPrefix_none_of_noop` ((WF14)), `clear_of_bot` (`site_not_bot`, (WF14)), and in the free direction `sitesOf_setAtE_bot`, `wf14_setAtE`, `lentPrefix_some_of_fill`. `clear_of_bot` takes (WF1) as a hypothesis (its three callers have `Good`). No statement weakened otherwise; no `sorry` added.
- The fact the walk lemma rests on is (WF1)'s first half, already a read-off clause swept EXACTLY: a value other than a borrow holds no borrow anywhere (`anyAtom` looks through marks, so no mark is directly over a borrow) and a borrow's content holds none, so every site at a borrow holder is under its `*`. Swept on this branch's checker: 70,526 states of the suite and 538 of the probes, no violation. The checker's rejection behind it is asserted beside an accepted control (`StarErasure.StarErasureProbe`: `let s = &r` for `r : &Nat` rejected, "a borrow of a borrow-typed place"; `let s = &*r` accepted).
- The records stay in `Regress`: `StarErasure.absorb_endLabel_fails` (now stated against the old `relend`, `relendOld`; `absorb_endLabel_now`: the same state under the new one keeps the invariant) and `updAt_bot_fails` (`sitesOf_updAt_bot` is stated by `walkKey`); `DepSites.dwsW_fails_SRb` (the invariant with sites compared step by step).

**The concrete states under the erased form** (kernel-checked, standard axioms). The six: the plain borrow, `Id1`, `Pick` at `Z`, `G8` after the re-rooting end (known and unknown `n`; the `StaticLend` checks of `relend` against the checker's `endBorrow` re-run under the new `relend` and pass), the conditional dependence, and the reborrow through `*` (`dws_SRb`). Two more (`Regress/DepSitesMore.lean`, the checker's states read at holes of its real runs, its sites the reference's): a reborrow of a reborrow, `let r = &a; let s = &*r; let t = &*s`, before and after the middle borrow's end (`dws_RR`, `dws_RR'`; the checker's `endBorrow` agrees with `relend` there, `rr_end`: `t` passes from `(s, [*])` to `(r, [*])`, no path holds two `*`s); and a field borrowed through a returned borrow of a pair, `let x = (a, b); let r = IdP(&x); let s = &(*r).0` (`dws_Pair`), the first state with a site BELOW a root by field, `(r, [*, .0])`: a read of `r` whose first field is `.1` reads field `1` whatever `s`'s final content is.

**A finding at the pair, for the lead.** `dws_Pair` holds when the type field `1` is read at does not read field `0` (`hty`; the library data model's `fieldTy` ignores the earlier fields). For a dependent pair it fails in the reading's TYPE (`dws_Pair_dependent_fails`, a conditional counterexample): `Sem.follow` types a field by its declaration at the earlier fields' meanings (`contentTyS`, `fieldHint`), and field `0` is lent, so two typed futures differing at its borrow read field `1` at different types; the meaning read is the same. Either the invariant compares the meanings only (the type of a field read being the closed future's, as point 5 e of D29 already has it for the checker's reads), or a borrow of a field that a later field's type reads is excluded by the checker's dependent-field rules; not decided here.

**The lead's decision on the pair: the values read.** The invariant is about what a read depends on, and a data value describes itself, so the value read needs no type to be found: `DepWithinSites` compares `(Sem.follow …).map Prod.fst`, and a field is typed at the closed future, as reads already are. The other way out (excluding a borrow of a field that a later field's type reads) is not an option: the checker deliberately lends such a field (the user's reading, with the dependent-values question in docs/32: inside a body a later field is typed by what it holds; D93's `LendIndex` is accepted). Built: the invariant restated on values; the value component of a reading reads no type (`follow_fst_indep`), and off field `0` the value read off a two-field constructor value does not read field `0`, whatever the declaration (`follow_dat_off`); `dws_Pair` holds with no hypothesis on the declaration; `dws_Pair_dependent_fails` is kept against the typed form (`DepWithinSitesT`) as the record. Checked on a dependent pair with its index lent: D93's `LendIndex` (`V := MkV(n : Word, x : Fin1(n))`, `let r = match *v { MkV(n, x) => &n }`), the checker's state read at a hole (the parameter's cell in frame `0`, `v ↦ borrow_0 MkV(loan_1, σ2)`, `r ↦ borrow_1 σ1`), `1` lent at `(v, [*, .0])`: reading `v` at `x` reads `σ2`'s meaning whatever the index's final content (`DepSitesMore.dws_LendIndex`, kernel-checked).

**Step 3, the risky clauses (2026-11-20).** The invariant is now in the core (`DepWithin.lean`: `readV`, `DepWithinSites`, `FreshReads`).
- **`callOf`'s two rows: proved.** The borrow-free row (`dws_take_fix`, `dws_callOfL_data`): the consumed holders are taken and every position reads the consumed labels fixed to constants. The borrow row (`dws_park_fix`, `dws_callOfL_ref`): a position that read a consumed label on a path comparable with the one read now reads the new label there, where it is lent as a fill; one that read none reads neither, and does not read the new label, which is at the counter (`FreshReads`: the positions read no label at or above the counter, from (I1) and `ShapeAt`). Hypotheses: `ShapeAt`, `FreshReads`, the borrow arguments' labels live.
- **`endLabel` with the new `relend`: two things are needed** (analysis, not built).
  1. *Readings by key.* The reference's `Sem.step` reaches a number's predecessor two ways, `.0` (key `(0, 0)`) and, on the partly moved form `S(⊥…)`, the constructor field `S.0` (key `(1, 0)`); the checker one way (`stepV`'s `.field` reaches no number). So the invariant is false at a reachable state through a path no place produces: after `let x = S(a); let r = &x.0` (accepted), `x` read at `[S.0]` is incomparable with `r`'s site by key and reads its final content (`Regress/StepKinds.lean`, `dws_fails_succField`, kernel-checked). Fix: a field step reads by its key (`Sem.stepKey`: `.proj i` and `.field g` naming one field read the same, a constructor field never reads a number), as the checker's walk does on the paths it accepts; then the value read at a path is a function of its key, and keys compose (a reading below a prefix is the reading of the prefix's value at the rest). A probe (reverted): 9 errors in `PlaceTyping`, `SiblingAccess`, `DepSites`, all unfoldings of `Sem.step`.
  2. *A plain site is a hole.* When `l` ends at its holder `r` and a position `q` reads `l` through a PLAIN site `z` strictly above the path read, the read depends on `l`'s final content only below `z`, so only on the borrows lent at `r` on paths comparable with the rest; `relend` gives those, and only those, sites comparable with the read. The invariant cannot say "only below `z`": it needs that the value at a plain site's path is the label's final content (`Hole`: `readK (valAt z.pos ψ) (key z.path) = some (ψ l)` for every live `l`, every plain site `z`). With it, the `endLabel` case splits: `l` not reached (unchanged); reached by a fill or at or below the read (every site at `r` re-rooted comparable with the read, so `r`'s whole value is fixed); reached through a plain site strictly above (the read is `r`'s read at the rest, by `Hole` and key composition). `Hole` is a second reference-only invariant: `borrowPlace` makes it, `callOf` keeps it (a consumed label's sites die; the others' holes are read at the fixed future), `endLabel` keeps it (a re-rooted plain site's value is `r`'s at the rest, the reborrow's hole there), a write keeps it after its access (no live plain site is comparable with the written path, and the old borrow of a borrow variable written whole ends first).
- **A write: one more alignment.** After its access no live label is lent comparably with the written path, so the value read there is fixed by labels that are not live, and the write is the same at every future the invariant compares only if `Sem.updAt` succeeds exactly where that reading is defined. `Sem.updAt` steps into a field by its index without checking the constructor (`Sem.step` checks it), so it must read by key too. With it the write keeps the invariant from the invariant alone: below the written path the value is the written one, above it the old value updated, elsewhere the old value.

Proposed, for the lead: readings and updates by key (`Sem.step`, `Sem.updAt`), and `Hole` beside the invariant; then the `endLabel` and write clauses as above.

### D68. Reading a place at a typed future instead of the closed one (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; the lead chose records only; built)

The lead's question: the chain relation at typed futures → closed future not typed mid-run → dependence within sites through every clause → `*` erasure → new `relend` → reads and writes by key → `Hole` exists to justify one move, reading a place at a typed future instead of the closed one. Could the reference's read simply BE at a typed future?

**(1) Where a reference reading at one future is transported to another.** Going through the run's clauses and every proof that applies the relation's readings clause (`Rel.sim`):
- **The closed-future reads: the only transport.** `contentOf` (every read, `readPlace`, a borrow's content), `tagOf` (a match's tag), `dropAt`'s read at the join, and `setContent`'s success check (the write is done per future, but whether it succeeds is decided at the closed future) all read at `s.closedFuture φ`. The proofs that relate them to the checker (`DropOne.content_follow`, `DropOneAsm`'s data read, and every read step to come) apply `Rel.sim` at the closed future (`hs.sim.1 _ hrel …` with `hrel : RelF φ s (s.closedFuture φ)`). These are exactly the two lemmas that broke when `Rel` moved to typed futures (D66), and dependence within sites was introduced only to move them to a typed future.
- **`semEnd` is not a transport of this kind.** [End] reads every position at `ψ.upd l (valAt r ψ)`; relating it to the checker's substitution applies `Rel` to the state before at that updated future, which is typed when the holder's content is a whole member of `l`'s held type at a typed future. That is the "a borrow's final content is a member" fact (D53 (h), [Repack], and the uniform rule being assessed on main: no borrow ends over a moved or partly moved content). Every route needs it; dependence within sites does not supply it.
- **None elsewhere.** `Rel.private_copy` (reference untouched, the checker's Ω the snapshot's), `sim_seq` (composition, per state), `Rel.start`: no future changes. The access's fixpoint reads sites only, then [End]. The write's meaning is per future. `callOf` reads the backward functions at the result's label, at the compared (typed) futures. `callOf_inPlace` compares the in-place run with the callee alone: their closed futures differ at the caller's labels, which no callee position reads (the frame property, not dependence within sites; with a typed future the same).

**(2) Definable, and the plumbing is `heldTy`'s.** Record, when a borrow is made, a member of its held type: `borrowPlace` the content it borrowed (`v`, read and checked whole there; the checker's [Borrow] rejects a place that is open, "[Repack] *v is borrowed whole, but it is open", probed on `V := MkV(n : Word, x : Fin1(n))`, so the content is a member, not only whole), `callOf`'s `.ref` row the forward value `L.enc c fwd` (a member of `L.toTy c` by construction). Both are computable `Sem`s, so the concrete runs still evaluate. A new field beside `heldTy` (or `heldTy` carrying the pair) is set at the same two places, with the same cascade through the records and the positional `SState.mk`s (the `heldTy` change touched about fifteen files, mechanically). Then the read is at `s.typedFuture φ`. Two forms:
- **The lead's**: each live label its holder's content where that content is a whole member, else the record. It needs the closed future's iteration (a holder's content may read other live labels), a classical membership test, and the congruence lemmas' (I2)-based argument (`closedFuture_agree`).
- **Simpler, and my recommendation**: each live label its record, `fun l => if s.Live l then rec l else φ l`. No iteration and no test: it agrees with `φ` off the live labels (`RelF`) and is typed by the records' membership (`TypedLive`) by construction; the run's congruence in `φ` (`run_congr`, today through `closedFuture_read`) becomes trivial, and `closedFuture_nonlive` is the definition. Which member is read matters only at a place that reads a live label it is not lent to, which is a fault either way; a place that reads its own borrow's content (a holder) reads it directly, not through the label.
The core lemmas keep their structure: the closed-future lemmas (`Locality.closedFuture_eq/agree/read`, `RunCongr`'s four uses, `DropOne.closedFuture_nonlive`) are replaced by simpler ones; the inhabitation clause of `Rel` (D66) becomes the records' membership, made where a label is made (`borrowPlace`, `callOf`) and untouched since (records and held types never change; a binding holder's stored type is the held type).

**(3) What is lost.** What the lead names: at a state where a place reads a live label it is not lent to, the reference's reading depends on the record, a model fault anyway. Two more, neither a loss for the simulation: the reference no longer reads "what the place holds now" through a holder at all (with records only), and the read step lemmas then need the checker's read value to mean the same at every compared future. That holds: after the access the content holds no loan of a live borrow (outside a sealed program, (WF2); a fill inside is ended by `accessInside`), so `meanV_congr` (proved, `Lemmas.lean`: a value's meaning reads the future only at its loans) makes its meaning one value at every future, and the reading at the typed future, which `Rel.sim` makes that meaning, is it. Dependence within sites, `Hole`, reads and writes by key are then not needed for the simulation; dependence within sites stays a true fact about the model, provable later or never. Nothing built is wasted: the erasure, the new `relend` and the two proved `callOf` rows stand, and reads by key remain a sound alignment.

**(4) Judgement: this route, with records only.** What it leaves: the record field and its plumbing (mechanical, done once already for `heldTy`); `typedFuture` and its trivial congruence; `content_follow` and the data read re-proved at the typed future, where `Rel.sim` applies directly; the read step lemmas through `meanV_congr`; the records' membership where a label is made (the one place with content, through [Borrow]'s [Repack] check). The other route needs `Hole` and dependence within sites through every clause of `run` (lets, matches and joins, calls and closures, the observation), reads and writes by key, and the path algebra under them, each a place for a new counterexample like the one at a number's predecessor. The one obligation both share, the [End] typedness above, is unchanged.

**The lead's decision (2026-11-20): records only.** The reference reads a place at the future that gives each live label the member recorded when the label was made and agrees with the run's future elsewhere. It is typed by construction, its congruence is trivial, and the relation at typed futures applies to it directly; the record matters only at a place that reads a live label it is not lent to, which no related state has.

**Built.**
- `SState.heldMem` beside `heldTy`, written where a label is made and never changed afterwards, at three places: `borrowPlace` (the content borrowed), `callOf`'s `.ref` row (the forward value `L.enc c fwd`), and a closure frame's borrow parameter (`bodyAt`, `fixStep`: the argument), the third found while carrying the field through.
- `SState.typedFuture s φ l = if l ∈ s.liveLabels then s.heldMem l else φ l`. `contentOf`, `tagOf`, `dropAt`'s read and `setContent`'s success check read at it; the run no longer reads the closed future. Its congruence in `φ` needs only (I1) (`Locality.typedFuture_read`).
- `Rel`'s inhabitation clause is now `records`: each live label's record is a whole member of its holder's content type (`holderTy`). The read's future is one the relation compares at (`relF_typedFuture`, `Rel.typedFuture_typed`), so `Rel.sim` applies to it directly.
- Re-proved at the typed future, standard axioms: `DropOne.content_follow` and `DropOneAsm`'s data read (through `Rel.sim` at the typed future and `meanV_congr`; the data read still rests on the open `dataMean_ne_proof`, as before), `Rel.start` (which takes the records' membership at the start state), `sim_seq`, `Rel.private_copy`, `botWrite_rel`, `rel_refs`, `rel_neutrals` and the access lemmas.
- The regression suite: every kernel-checked run still evaluates and agrees with the checker's verdicts (`StaticLend`, `CallInPlace`, `EntryCheck`, `OneCtorEntry`, `CodeRuns`, `DataCodes`, `DepSites`, `DepSitesMore`, `CE4_flipped`, `CE5_flipped`, `IdUnitWrite`, `StarErasure`, `StepKinds` and the rest, with the attack files `RunEqs`, `CloseCall`, `CloseCallBack`). No result changed. The only statement changes are the added records: the attack's states `S₃`…`S₆` take the record `c`, `CallInPlace`'s frames the record `r`.
- `PartMove` (`Regress/PartMoveRead.lean`, kernel-checked): at the read of `q` in `let r = &p; let z = (*r).0; let w = q; (*r).0 := z; w`, on the checker's state read at a hole of its real run, the typed future gives `r`'s label its record, `p`'s pair. It is typed when the record is a whole member of `r`'s type (`typed_at_read`), the read is `q`'s meaning (`read_q`), and that is the checker's value's meaning at every future (`checker_q`). The closed future, where the read was taken until now, is not typed there (`closed_not_typed`).

**The records' membership** (`Steps/Records.lean`), the only obligation with content.
- `borrowPlace`: the record is the content borrowed, whole by `borrowPlace`'s own check, held at the type `contentOf` read it at (`borrowPlace_record`, proved). Its membership, `borrowPlace_record_mem` (open), uses on the checker's side: for a whole place, the checker's `Membership` at the position, with wholeness from [Borrow]'s rejection of a partly moved place and [Repack]'s rejection of a whole borrow of an open value; for a field, the parent's partial membership (a whole member with parts moved out) and the data model's `pack_isSome` with `pack_fields` (each field of an element is a member of its field type at the earlier fields), the field being read at its content's own type, a number at the declaration's field type.
- `callOf`'s `.ref` row: the record is `L.enc c fwd` held at `L.toTy c` (`callOfL_record`, proved). It is a member by the encoding at every level (`enc_mem_lvl0/1/2`, proved, nothing from the checker). It is whole when the model's reading gives whole fields (`DataRead.WholeFields`; `enc_whole_lvl0/1/2`, proved from it): a fact about the data model, owed where the reading is fixed, beside `genericModel`'s coherence.
- The closure frame's record belongs to the closure meaning's own run, which no checker state is related to, so the clause is not asked of it.
- The checker's side, as verdicts the build asserts (`Regress/RecordSites.lean`): a whole borrow of an open value is rejected by [Repack] in three forms on `V := MkV(n : Word, x : Fin1(n))` (the index written, then the pair borrowed whole; borrowed, then written back; written back to an acceptable index before the borrow, since the field's type is the stuck `Fin1(σ1)`), beside the accepted whole borrow with nothing written. A borrow of the dependent field is accepted, and rewriting the index while it is borrowed is rejected at the end. There is no syntactic shadow to sweep: the record is taken when the label is made, and a content can become open only afterwards, by a write that [Repack] checks when the borrow ends.

The [End] typedness obligation both routes shared is the rule of main's D98 (D66 above): no borrow ends over a moved-out content, in an observation as anywhere. Next: the call steps (`callOf_inPlace`, with the frame property as its own lemma), then the assemblies.

### D69. A callee that returns its borrow parameter: the result keeps a label issued before the call (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; for the lead)

`IdP (x : &Nat) : &Nat := x` with `let y = 2; let r = IdP(&y); *r := 5; y` is accepted. After the call the checker holds `y ↦ lent_0 loan_0` and `r ↦ borrow_0 2` (`Regress/ReturnParam.lean`, at a hole of its real run): `callFn` runs the body in place, the body returns the parameter's borrow `0`, which `&y` made before the call, and `.ended g [0]` hands the result's marks to it. The reference applies the callee's meaning, whose borrow row returns a fresh label, the counter (`Steps.callOfL_ref_label`, proved), and consumes the argument's label. So after `let r = IdP(&y)` the checker's `r` holds `0` and the reference's holds a label that did not exist before the call; any renaming relating the two states sends `0` to it (`ReturnParam.result_not_old`). Part (a) of the main theorem asks the renaming after a step to agree with the one before on every label the checker had issued (`SimConcl`/`SimConclT`: `∀ l < st.nextLoan, π' l = π l`), which is false here. False with it: `callFn_step_stmt` and every statement that runs a call, and `callOf_inPlace` as stated (its renaming fixes every label below the counter, while the in-place result is the argument's label).

Two fixes, for the lead:
- (B) The checker: the result of a borrow-returning call is always a fresh borrow, the reserved `g` (as `callFn`'s comment already says: "`g` becomes the result's label"): the borrow the body returned is ended into `g`, its marks and loans becoming `g`'s. Then every borrow-returning call returns a new label, whether it runs (`runBody`), closes off (`closeCall`, already fresh) or is the meaning applied (`callOf`, fresh), and the argument's label occurs nowhere after the call on either side. The reference's in-place run (`runCallRef`) does the same, and `callOf_inPlace` keeps its renaming form (fixing every label below the counter). A checker change, main's lane.
- (A) The statement: the renaming after a step agrees with the one before only on the labels whose images are still live in the reference's state (the argument's image is consumed by `callOf`). Composable (a dead label stays dead), but it changes part (a) of the main theorem, and the in-place run and the meaning then differ in which label the result has, a renaming the call step has to make by hand.
Recommended: (B), the uniform form: a returned borrow is a new borrow on every path, as it already is on two of the three.

**(B) decided and landed: main's D99 (874584e1).** A borrow-returning call returns the fresh borrow it reserves, whether the body ran or the call closed off; the borrow the body returned (or [Close]'s `k`) is ended into it, its loans and marks becoming the reserved label's (`LendOp.into`). The checker now does what `runCallRef` mirrors (reserve, lend at the borrow arguments' lenders, run in place, rename into the reserved label). After the rebase: `ReturnParam`'s guards show `IdP(&y)`'s result at the reserved `1` with the argument's `0` gone, and `Pick2(&x, &y)`'s at the reserved `2`; the checker states read at holes in `StaticLend` (`G8`, `G8K`, `PZ`), `DepSites` and `DepSitesMore` (`Pair`) have the result at the reserved label (`G8`: `3` to `2`; `G8K`: `1` to `2`; `PZ`: `0` to `2`, counter `3`; `Pair`: `2` to `1`), relabelled with every check passing; `CallAsm.callTailM` is D99's code.

**And the reference's in-place run lent its result too little (fixed).** `CallRef.runCallRef` kept the sites the pop's [End]s gave the returned borrow, made fills. For `Pick2 (a : &Nat) (b : &Nat) : &Nat := &*a` those are `a`'s lender's only, while the checker lends the result at every borrow argument's lender (its marks `lent_g`, handed to the result by `.ended g [k]`: after `let r = Pick2(&x, &y)` it holds `x ↦ lent_3 loan_3`, `y ↦ lent_3 2`, and reading `y` ends `r`, `UsePick2Read` rejected; `Regress/ReturnParam.lean`), as `callOf` does (`lentK`). Now `runCallRef` sets the result's sites to every place a borrow argument was lent at before the call, as fills. The two concrete in-place calls still agree with `callOf` (`inc_agree`; `id1_inPlace_typed`, its lending now read off the arguments' sites).

### D70. The frame property of the reference's run: stated, checked on the two concrete calls (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; for `callOf_inPlace`)

`callOf_inPlace` compares the body run in place (the callee's frame pushed on the caller's state, `CallRef.runCallRef`) with the body run in the closure's own frame (`callOf`'s meaning: one frame, each borrow parameter at a label of its own whose CELL, a temporary, reads the label's final content). Its first ingredient, the frame property, is now a named statement (`FrameProp.run_frame`, open): from related states a term runs to related states with the same result, or fails on both sides.

**The relation** (`FrameRel s₀ ks s s'`, for the in-place state at the call `s₀` and the parameter labels `ks` in cell order). The closure has one frame; in place the caller's frames lie below the callee's. The top frames hold the same bindings; the closure's temporaries are the cells, then the in-place ones (`upPos`: an in-place top position `.bind d i` is the closure's `.bind 0 i`, `.temp d j` is `.temp 0 (j + c)`). The caller's frames keep their structure and holders and read what they read at the call, at the future that gives each parameter label its cell's value (`lowerVal`, `cellFuture`): ending a parameter's label in place updates the caller's lent places where the closure updates the cell, and a call's fixing of its consumed arguments does both alike. Shapes, top-frame sites, records and the counter agree (a parameter's held type is not compared: in place it is the caller's, in the closure none, and the run reads no held type). The caller's borrows keep their sites; sites of the frame's borrows in the caller's frames are not compared (the closure has none, and nothing the run does reads them: an [Access] at a top position reaches no site below, and `runCallRef` lends the result afresh). The starting conditions (`FrameStart`): the caller's frames read no label at or above the counter, the parameter labels are below it, distinct, and held in the top frame only. Labels are the same on both sides: the closure's own labels are renamed to the in-place ones by the counter lemma (`run_counter_indep`), generalised.

**Checked on the two concrete calls** (`Regress/FrameCheck.lean`, kernel-checked, standard axioms), for any `self` binding (the property compares frames with the same bindings; that the closure's meaning binds `self` to the closure itself is the fixpoint's part of `callOf_inPlace`): `Id1(&y)` (`id1_frame`: the starting conditions, the states at the call related, both runs giving `a` at the reborrow `1` with the same type, the states after related, the reborrow parked and lent at `*x` on both sides and `y` reading the parameter's label, which the cell reads), and `Inc(&y)` (`inc_frame`, with `self` as the call in place binds it: `()` on both sides, related after).

**`Id` is excluded** (`Term.idFree`). An `Id`'s observation reads every position after ending every live borrow: in place it reads the caller's frames, where the closure reads the cells. The two propositions agree when the caller's frames determine the cells' values, which holds where each parameter label's final content is a member of its type at the observation: a place lent to a returned borrow reads its final content decoded through the backward function, which is injective on members but sends every non-member to the error value. At accepted programs that holds (main's D98: no borrow ends over a moved-out content, in an observation as anywhere; a typed body writes members), but the reference alone does not know it, so the property at an `Id` needs that as a hypothesis on the run, discharged from the checker's acceptance of the callee's body. Not yet stated.

**What `callOf_inPlace` then needs besides the frame property**: the counter lemma (labels); the self binding (the in-place frame binds `self` to the callee's reading, the closure's meaning to `err` (non-recursive) or the stage's closure (recursive): a non-recursive body that never reads `self`, and for a recursive one the fixpoint equation under [Rec]); the pop against the closure's ends and finals (`popCallFrame` ends the frame's labels but the returned one and moves its holder to the caller; `bodyAt` ends the same labels, and the cells, read after, are what `callOf` writes into the lent places, `lowerVal` at the end); and the lending, now `runCallRef`'s own (D69). With D69 open, the returned label's renaming waits on the lead's decision.

### D71. Calls at the statement level: the frame property with the renaming, D99's call in place, the assemblies (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; the lead's step 1 of breadth before depth)

**The frame property and the renaming are one simulation** (`FrameProp.lean`): `FrameRel σ s₀ ks` relates the in-place state with the closure's frame through an injective renaming `σ` of the closure's labels (its counter onwards to the in-place counter onwards); `run_frame` (open) for a term with no `Id` or a frame with no parameter. `run_counter_indep` is now its corollary (moved from `Lemmas`, proved from `run_frame`: no caller frame, no parameter, the shift between the counters; one hypothesis added, the bindings' types carry no label). The `Id` gap is stated as `run_frame_obs` (open) under `CellsDetermined` and `CellsAtObs`. `Regress/FrameCheck` still checks `Inc` and `Id1` (identity renaming).

**D99 in the reference** (decided by the lead: a borrow-returning call that runs returns the fresh borrow it reserves): `runCallRef` reserves the result's label when the codomain is a borrow, lends it where the borrow arguments lent, runs the body in place, and renames the returned borrow into it (`SState.endInto`). `Regress/CallInPlaceD99.id1_inPlace_typed` re-checks `Id1(&y)` in place against `callOf` under it (kernel-checked). `callOf_inPlace` is restated against it: the same result, its label included, and the same renaming; `callOf_inPlace_rel` is its form at a call related to the checker's, for every body (the `Id` hypotheses discharged from the checker's run: D98, typing).

**The assemblies** (`Steps/CallAsm.lean`; no `sorry` in their bodies, each leaf a named `sorry` with its one-paragraph statement): `runBody_step` (front, the body by the induction hypothesis in tail position, back, the run in place re-read under the final assignment), `nestedRun_sim` (the nested start related to the empty state by `Rel.start`, the step at every future, one assignment for all), `callFn_step` (front with the reservation, the call proper by `closeCall_step` or `runBody_step`, the tail), `closeCall_step` (checks, the fills by induction one at a time, the result). They are now the proofs of `Call.runBody_step`, `callFn_step`, `closeCall_step` and `nestedRun_sim`.

**Found by writing them** (all fixed in the open):
1. *The induction hypothesis ran every term out of tail position.* `eval_step_stmt`, `evalCore_step_stmt` and `letIn_step_stmt` compared the checker with `run` (`runT false`), while a body runs in tail position on both sides (`runBody` sets `tailNext`; `runBodyRef` runs `runT true`). Now the reference's tail flag is the checker state's (`runT … st.tailNext`). Backed by the assembly: the body's step has no other hypothesis to use.
2. *The simulation form kept the context and concluded no `CtxOk`.* `SimConcl`, `SimConclT`, `eval_step_stmt`, `evalCore_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` and `nfSealed_step_stmt` (with `nestedRun_sim`) now conclude in the extended form the join's statements already had (`Δ` extended at new unknowns, `CtxOk` after, the tables only grow): without it no step could follow another (the pop after a body).
3. *The value a step returns was not counted.* The same statements related the checker's state without the returned value; the reference parks a returned borrow in a temporary (D14), and the relation matches temporaries by the borrows they hold, so after `&x` nothing matched (`Regress/InFlight.not_rel_without_flight`, kernel-checked: the reference's state after `&y` is related to no checker state whose frame holds no borrow in a temporary, which is the checker's after its `borrowPlace`). Now the checker's state is read with the value in flight (`MState.withTemp`, `GoodF` with `flightOf`), as the borrow statements already did.
4. *A body runs at one fuel less than the hypothesis.* `runBody` at `fuel + 1` evaluates its body at `fuel - 1`, the induction hypothesis is at `fuel`. Decided: strong induction in the knot (`StepsIHBelow`, `Knot.stepsIH_below`); the hypothesis is at every fuel up to the step's.
5. *The nested run needs one assignment for every future* (`nested_uniform`, a leaf): the induction hypothesis gives an assignment per future, the statement one for all; a loan-free text run from an empty frame reads the future only at labels it makes, so the values of the unknowns it creates do not depend on it.
6. *`callFn`'s hypotheses must supply [Close]'s readings and the run in place's success* (`callFn_front`): `closeCall_step_stmt` needs the row, the declared-`Unit` readings and the cells' types tied to the code, `runBody_step_stmt` that the run in place succeeds; `callFn_step_stmt` has neither, so they are the front's obligations (the decisions' alignment; the callee's meaning a member of its type).

**The rule for every step statement from now on** (the lead's decision, 2026-11-20): a statement in
the simulation form concludes in the extended form and counts the value it returns as in flight:
an assignment and a `Δ` extending the old ones at the unknowns the step created
(`∀ σ < st.absTy.size, ρ' σ = ρ σ ∧ Δ' σ = K.Δ σ`), a renaming agreeing on the labels issued, the
tables only growing (`st.absTy.size ≤ st'.absTy.size`, `st.nextLoan ≤ st'.nextLoan`), `CtxOk` of the
state after under the extended context, the relation and `GoodF` of the state after **with the
returned value counted** (`st'.withTemp v`, `flightOf r.2`). `SimConcl` and `SimConclT` are that
form; the remaining statements in the old form are migrated with their areas, and no new statement is
written in it.

**The decisions are extended with `Δ`** (decided 2026-11-20, after the slice prover's finding that
`access_ctx` is false: an access can grow the table of unknowns, an end's [Repack] making the lent
part a fresh unknown, and the context's decisions, fixed, cannot agree with the checker's on inputs
naming it). The extended context is `{ K with Δ := Δ', D := D' }`, with a new decision table `D'`
that agrees with the old one on every input naming only unknowns that existed before the step,
`K.D.AgreeOld st.absTy.size D'` (`Decisions.AgreeOld`: places and readings name no unknown and agree
outright; a term's decision agrees when its unknowns are below the bound; `refl`, `mono`, `trans`).
The other form offered (keep `D`, restrict `CtxOk`'s decision clause to inputs over existing
unknowns, the extension a separate fact) was not taken: it would weaken `CtxOk` itself, so every
reader of the decisions would carry a side condition on the unknowns of its input, while extending
`D` with `Δ` is the left half of D57/D65 completed in the one place the context grows. The old
inputs keep their decisions (`decAligned_grow`, and `runT_congrD` for a run that reads only old
inputs). Applied to every statement in the extended form (`SimConcl`, `SimConclT`, `eval`,
`evalCore`, `letIn`, `nfSealed`, `observe`, `idType`, the call statements and their assemblies,
the chain's `const_value`); `movedPlace`, `dropOne` and the join's drop get it when the slice
branch is merged. The induction hypothesis is at every fuel up to the step's (`StepsIHBelow`, the knot by
strong induction: the lead's decision on item 4 below, in place of a fuel-monotonicity fact about the
checker).

**Running count, per area** (for the lead's tracking in docs/32):

| Area | Checker faults | Model definition changes | Relation changes | Statements found false |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Calls (step 1, D69–D71) | 1 (D69: a running call kept the argument's label; D99, a checker change) | 1 (`runCallRef`: lent too little, then D99's reservation and renaming) | 0 to `Rel` (the frame relation is new, for calls) | 4 (part (a)'s renaming at `IdP`; the tail flag; the extended context; the value in flight) |

### D72. The top-level chain assembled; the knot of [Def] (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; area 2a)

**Assembled, no `sorry` in the bodies** (`Meaning/Consistency.lean`): `consistencyBounded_assembled` (from `consistency_rel` and the existence of a data model, resolution being a function of the program), `consistency_rel` (a parameterless definition of type `False` would mean a member of `prop False`, a proof of `False`), `defn_member` (by induction on the items, one at a time), `step_member` (an inductive declaration adds no definition; a definition is `step_member_const` or `step_member_fix`), and `step_member_const`: the check taken apart (`const_split`), the body by `eval_step` from the start related to the empty state, [Return]'s membership by `bodyReturns_sound` in the extended context with the value in flight, the constant's meaning that value's (`const_value`). The induction hypothesis there is the knot's (`Knot.stepsIH`, every fuel). The data model's faithfulness (the data-model lane's finding 1) is a named hypothesis of the chain, `DataFaithful`, an opaque placeholder until that lane's statement replaces it and enters `CtxOk`; `exists_dataModel` now owes it at every prefix.

**The knot of [Def]: the chain cannot be closed for a function** (`Regress/DefKnot.ctx_contains_fix_member`, kernel-checked). `checkDef` pushes a function's global before it runs `checkFix`, so that recursive calls find it. `checkFix_sound_stmt` asks `CtxOk` at that state, and `CtxOk`'s `GlobalsOk` asks every global of the state to mean the closure of its code (`fn`), at its stored type (`ty`), and to be a member of it (`mem`): for the global being defined that is `checkFix_sound`'s conclusion. So `checkFix_sound` is vacuous at a definition, and no statement proves an accepted function a member of its type (`step_member_fix`, the leaf the chain stops at). Every step lemma run inside the body assumes the same `CtxOk`, so the circularity is not local to `checkFix_sound`. What breaks it: the guarded recursion on the decreasing argument, by induction on its rank: `CtxOk` asks of the definition being checked only what the recursion gives, membership at arguments of smaller rank (or its meaning the stage before, `RunCongr.fixStage`, with that stage's membership), and `checkFix_sound` is proved by induction on the rank bound. That changes `GlobalsOk` for the definition being checked; not done, for the lead. The rank itself is changing (the data-model lane's finding 2: rank as the height of the value's meaning), so its form waits on that port.

**Also found by assembling**: `bodyReturns_sound_stmt` asked `Good` of the state without the value it checks; after the in-flight rule (D71) the step before it gives `GoodF` of the state with the value counted, and `Good` of the state without it is false when the value is a borrow (its loan has no borrow in the state). Restated with the value in flight (`GoodF … (st.withTemp v) fl`, its typed future `TypedFutureF`); the constant's assembly is what uses it.

**Open leaves of the chain**: `accepted_resolves`, `resolves_of_accepted`, `resolvesTo_unique`, `covered_prefix` (checker facts about the program loop); `falseConst_ty`, `false_means_empty`; `exists_dataModel` (now with faithfulness); `const_split`, `typedFutureF_exists`, `const_value`; `step_member_fix` (the knot).

| Area | Checker faults | Model definition changes | Relation changes | Statements found false |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Top-level chain (2a, D72) | 0 | 0 | 0 to `Rel`; one to `CtxOk` proposed (`GlobalsOk` for the definition being checked) | 2 (`checkFix_sound` vacuous at a definition; `bodyReturns_sound` without the value in flight) |

### D73. The knot of [Def]: design note (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; paper only, built after the data-layer port)

The plan's outer induction, on the size of the decreasing argument, missing from the Lean statements (D72). What changes, and what the rank must provide.

**1. The clause for the definition being checked.** The run context names the definition being checked and a bound: `RunCtx.cur : Option (String × Nat)` (its name and `b`). `GlobalsOk` asks of every other global what it asks now (`fn`, `ty`, `mem`). Of the global named by `cur` it asks `fn` and `ty` as now (its meaning is the closure of its code, its full guarded fixpoint, at its stored type) and, in place of `mem`, membership BELOW the bound: `MemBelow b f T`, the closure applied at any member arguments whose decreasing component has rank below `b` is defined, its result a member of the codomain at them and its finals members of the borrow parameters' types. A non-recursive definition (`dec = none`) is asked nothing at `cur` (its body never calls itself, [Rec]). `cur` enters at `checkDef`'s push of the global; before it, and for an earlier global, nothing changes. The full closure, not a stage, is the global's meaning: the stages appear only inside the proof (below). A closure whose self binding holds the closure being checked (a local `fix`) is the same with `Membership` in place of `GlobalsOk` (item 5).

**2. `checkFix_sound` by induction on the bound.** `P(b)`: `MemBelow b g Π` for the definition's closure `g`. `P(0)` is vacuous. `P(b) → P(b + 1)`: take member arguments whose decreasing component has rank at most `b`; the assignment of the generic state that gives each parameter's unknown its argument is typed (`generic_good`); `CtxOk` holds at the generic state under `cur := (f, b)` (earlier globals by the chain's induction, `f` itself by `P(b)`); the generic run (`Rel.start`, `checkTail_step` along the case tree, the return check by `conv_sound` and `bodyReturns_sound`) gives a leaf whose value is a member of the goal and is the reference's run of the body in the closure's frame at those arguments (the frame property, D70, with `self` the closure). That run is `g` at the arguments, because `g args = stage (rank args + 1) args` and every call it makes to itself is at a smaller rank (item 3), where `g` and the stage agree. The full meaning: every argument's decreasing component has some finite rank `r`, and `P(r + 1)` covers it; so `g` is defined and a member at every member argument, `mkTele` succeeds, and the closure is a member of its Π-type. `recCheck_sound_stmt` (which states the whole membership on the closure value) is replaced by this induction and the call-step statement of item 3.

**3. A recursive call at the call step.** Under `cur := (f, b)`, a recursive call (through `self` or the global) passes a decreasing argument that [Rec] (`recCheck`, run by `callFn` in the typed generic run) accepted as a STRICT PART of the entry value: a field of a field … of the parameter's value as it was passed, through matches, `let`s and borrows. A new statement supplies its meaning: `recDecreases_stmt`, from a related state, a successful `recCheck` means the decreasing argument's meaning is a strict part of the entry argument's meaning, under the assignment and at every typed future. With the rank's property below, its rank is below the entry's, hence below `b`, so `MemBelow b` gives the callee's membership there (what `runBody_step`'s `hok` and `callFn_front` need: the run in place succeeds and is `callOf`). **What the rank must satisfy** (for the data-model lane): (R1) it is defined on every meaning (whole, partly moved, open: the entry value at a recursive call is often partly moved, `match x { Cons(h, t) => f(t) }`); (R2) a strict part has strictly smaller rank, through any nesting (fields of fields; through a nested declaration's parameters, a forest inside a tree: the finding at `List(Rose(Nat))`), whatever the parts moved out of the whole; (R3) a member's rank is its element's (the run decodes and re-encodes members: `callOf` passes the arguments' meanings, `bodyAt` decodes them); (R4) the entry's rank is taken at the call's start, the argument's when it is passed, and (R2) relates them (a part moved out of the entry before the recursive call is still a part of the entry as it was passed).

**4. Which statements change.** Every statement that assumes `CtxOk` changes its hypothesis (all of `StepsIH`), since `GlobalsOk` is part of it, and `RunCtx` gains `cur`. Proofs that could notice: those that read a global's membership or meaning: the call steps (`callFn` on a `.gfn` or `self` callee: `fres`, `hok`, `callOf_inPlace_rel`; `runBody` through `fixOf`), `evalCore`'s `.const` (reads `K.C.global`), `funType`/`valType` of a global, `recCheck`, and `conv` where it compares function values. Proofs that cannot: the drop at the join and the leaves under it (they read places, sites and declared readings, never a global's membership), the borrow steps, [End], [Access], substitution. The slice prover's leaves (`dataMean_ne_proof`, `access_root_not_proof`, `access_ctx`, `copyRead_aligned`, `runs_valTypeDecl_keep`) read no global: their statements change only in carrying the new `CtxOk`, their proofs not at all.

**5. Local closures and function parameters.** A local `fix` (a closure formed in a body): `checkFix` at the fix site runs its body generically with its captures; its `self` binding holds the closure value, whose meaning is the full closure (`meanV`'s `.clo`, the guarded fixpoint by stages). `Good`'s `Membership` asks that binding to be a member of its Π-type, the same circularity as `GlobalsOk.mem`; the same fix: the self binding of the closure being checked is asked `MemBelow b`, the bound in the context beside `cur`, and the induction of item 2 proves its membership. A non-recursive local closure needs no bound. A closure value met later (captured, passed, returned) is a member by the step that formed it (`capture_step`, the `.fix` clause's reading), not by a new induction. A function parameter (an unknown of a Π-type, or a borrow of one): its meaning is the assignment's, a member by `TypedAssignment`; nothing to prove and no recursion through it.

### D74. `Id`: the assembly, a concrete check, the bridge to the frame property (fifth lead, 2026-11-20; area (d))

**Statements in the final form.** `observe_step_stmt` and `idType_step_stmt` conclude in the extended form (D71's rule): the tables grow; under every typed extension (`Δ` extended at the unknowns the sides and [Obs-ref] create) the state after meets `CtxOk`, is related, and is good (`idType`'s with its result in flight).
