**Found by the assembly**: `observe_step_stmt` said nothing about the state its side leaves, and `idType` observes the second side FROM that state (the first `observe` keeps the counters, records and new unknowns: D23); so the second observation had no `Good`, `CtxOk` or relation to start from. Now concluded, for every typed extension (statement found false: incomplete, the composition impossible).

**The assembly** (`Steps/IdAsm.lean`, `idType_step_asm`, now `Obs.idType_step`'s proof; no `sorry` in the body): under a typed extension the checker's conjunction holds exactly when the two reference observations at the written values are equal: the two observations by `observe_step` (the second from the first's end state), each position off the footprint by `footprint_coverage` (the [Id-typestate] hypotheses from the split), on it by `observe_step`'s owners, the list equality by `obs_eq_iff` (proved), the conjunction's meaning by the split (`mkEqM_step` inside it); the reference's `Id` is that equality at every typed written value (`id_run`), each realised by some typed extension (`fresh_realize`, [Obs-ref]); so the `Id`'s two directions (true under every typed extension when the reference's is; equivalent under one). Leaves: `idType_split`, `id_run`, `fresh_realize`, `footprint_positions`.

**Concrete** (`Regress/IdWrite.lean`): `IdW` (both sides write `S(Z)` through `r = &y`) accepted, `IdWNe` (`S(Z)` against `S(S(Z))`) rejected, `IdWMove` (one side moves `z`) rejected by [Id-typestate]; kernel-checked on the end states the sides leave: the reference's observations are equal when the writes agree (`obs_same`), differ at `y` when they do not (`obs_differ`), and `z`, outside the footprint, reads alike after either side (`obs_z_same`). The [Obs-ref] case at `Unit` and `Nat` is `Regress/IdUnitWrite.lean` (D26).

**The bridge to the frame property's `Id` case** (`cells_of_observe`, a named leaf): inside a callee's body run in place, a side the checker observed leaves, in the closure's run, cells holding whole members at the observation (`CellsAtObs`, from D98 and typing) and the caller's places tell them apart (`CellsDetermined`): the two hypotheses of `FrameProp.run_frame_obs`, which `callOf_inPlace_rel` needs for a body with an `Id` and a borrow parameter.

| Area | Checker faults | Model definition changes | Relation changes | Statements found false |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Id` (d, D74) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 (`observe_step` said nothing of its end state) |

"Agree" means the same effects, order and result under the aligned decisions; "below" means the checker also ends or drops something the run leaves, which the order `Below` allows. The checker's side of each program is asserted by the build of `Regress/AdequacyProbes.lean` (`AdequacyProbes2`, the accepted program beside a rejected control where one can be written). The run's side is **read off the clause, not kernel-checked**, for every row below; rows fixed earlier (D19, D21) have their kernel-checked records there.

**Summary: 36 cases. 19 agree (5 of them only since D19/D21, 4 up to "below"); 3 are the statements' business; 14 carry one or more of fifteen divergences A1–A15.** In the order of how much accepted code each breaks:

- **A9, A8** (rows 13, 21, 23): an unannotated pair or `Cons`, a bare `Nil` as an argument or a result: the run fails. Nearly every program over a parameterised type.
- **A1** (rows 3, 4, 5, 8, 9): [Access] ends what the whole variable's meaning depends on, where the checker ends what the place read (or, for a match, the tag) depends on. The run ends a borrow the checker keeps, and a later write through it is lost.
- **A3** (row 10): a match on a proof with other than one arm fails; it should be `⋆` unrun, as every proof is.
- **A5** (row 28): `Id` is false in the run whenever its sides park different numbers of temporaries, which is whenever one borrows and the other does not.
- **A7, A10, A11, A12** (rows 33, 19, 23, 34): a function or a `D(ā)` loses its structure through an annotation, a closure value's captures, a call's result, a field.
- **A2** (row 5): D67's owned access. D19's R5 put the right-hand side first, which is right, and exposed this: the full access then ends the reborrow the right-hand side just made.
- **A13** (rows 4, 5, 35): open dependent values have no meaning in the model.
- **A4, A6, A14, A15**: a join after a by-type match, the join's entry set read after the arm, `clone`/`peek` of a partly moved place, the label counter. No accepted program found that shows them; A6 and A4 change which places are `moved`, which `Id` and `Eq` observe.

**Batch status (kept current at every commit).** The batch goes in four stages, the core lemmas re-proved after each (`#print axioms`: the standard three).

- Stage 1, DONE: **A2** (`.assign` skips the access at a holder root written whole; the old borrow's end carries the content back), **A3** (`SM.erasedIf`: every wrapped clause is `•` unrun at `flags.2`, as `evalAt` erases; kernel-checked `Regress/Adequacy_flipped.erased_unrun`), **A4** (no join after a match on a proof), **A5** (`observation` reads the positions the side STARTED with), **A6** (`joinDrop` takes the entry's moved places, read before the arm), **A7** (`.ascribe`, `.eq`, `.id` hand on the annotation's shape and data; `.tind` records its data; a parameter binding keeps its domain's data). A cost of A3: a term's run now depends on the erasure flag, so `Program.lean`'s sanity lemmas (`defMeaning_one` …) assume the decisions do not erase `S(Z)`.
- Stage 2, DONE: **A8** (`PiParam.data`: a call's argument is run at its parameter's type WITH its data, so a bare `Nil` argument reads its parameters; a closure's body is run at its codomain with its structure, `RunCongr.fixCod`, so a bare `Nil` result does), **A9** (`runFields` threads the solved parameters, `Machine.evalCtor`'s `sol`: the written ones or the hint's first, then each field's returned type fills the unsolved ones, `Run.unifyTyS`; a field is run at its declared type once the parameters that type reads are solved; kernel-checked `Adequacy_flipped.ctor_no_hint` and four `unifyTyS` examples). Residual, as rows A11/A12: the inference reads the data a field's run returns, which a call's result and a field read do not carry, and it does not look for a parameter nested two deep (`List(Pair(A, B))`).
- Stage 3, DONE: **A10** (`capsTys` binds each capture at its recorded type WITH its structure: `tyShape` and `tyDataV`, two functions in the run's mutual block, read a stored Π-type's shape off its term in its captures' frame and a stored `D(ā)`'s parameters; kernel-checked `Adequacy_flipped.captured_pi_has_shape`) and a sixteenth divergence found while planning, **A16**: `MState.sstate` bound each checker binding at `Value.tyS` of its stored type, which had no Π-shape, so from `SM' K ρ st`, the state every step statement starts from, a call through a function-typed binding failed. Fixed: `CodeMeaning.tyShape` (the run's `tyShape` at the constant future) is read by `Value.tyS`; a deferred parameter type (`CodeMeaning.paramTy`) is the parameter's binder with its structure; `deltaOf` and `globalTyS` are now `Value.tyS` (kernel-checked `stored_pi_has_shape`). **Cost, a restated hypothesis:** `FillShape` (the substitution pair's hypothesis on the filler) gains "the filler is not itself a Π-type or `D(ā)` type value". A loan standing as a recorded type has no structure to read and its filler would; no stored type holds a live loan, so this never arises in a reachable state, but the pair is proved for every term. RESIDUAL: [End] of a borrow of a type-valued place holding a Π-type or `D(ā)`; the fix is the hypothesis "no recorded type of the term holds the loan" in place of this one.
- Stage 4, DONE (but NOT fixed for packed data until D25: `ctorM` meant `err` at an ill-typed future, which made a read of one field depend on a lent sibling's label): **A1**. `SState.access s p o` takes an observation `o : Sem → Sem` of the position's meaning and ends the live labels what `o` observes depends on (`SState.obsDep`; at `o = id` it is the old access). A read, a borrow, an assignment and a drop observe the content at the place's path with its type (`Run.obsAt … wholeOb`), so reading `x.b` leaves a borrow of `x.a` live; a match observes the tag only (`Run.tagOf` with `natTag`/`indTag`, replacing `contentOf` in the two match clauses), so the borrows inside a scrutinee's fields stay live until an arm reads the field. `access_FT` (the fact the congruence in the future rests on) now says: after the access, what `o` observes factors through the non-live labels; `contentOf_congr` gets the content back from it (`follow_of_wholeOb`), `tagOf_congr` is it directly. Kernel-checked: `Adequacy_flipped.access_ends_nothing` (an observation that reads no live label ends nothing) and `access_other_field` (the pair instance: observing the second component ends nothing, the whole meaning reads the label). **Two statements were false and are restated:** `access_step_stmt` claimed the checker's state below the reference's full access for every `owned`, false at a borrow variable written whole (the checker's owned access ends nothing there: A2 at the statement level); `assignPlace_step_stmt` was against `setContent` after a full access, missing the old borrow's end and the new one's hold. Both are now against what the run does: the `.assign` clause's tail is the named function `Run.assignAt`, and the statement is stated against it.

**After the batch.** Fixed: A1–A10 and A16. Still rows, each its own redesign: **A11** (a call's result has no Π-shape or data: `PiOut` carries a `TyM`), **A12** (a field read has no structure: the data model's field types are `TyM`s), **A13** (open dependent values have no meaning), **A14** (`clone`/`peek` of a partly moved place), **A15** (the label counter after a private copy). New residuals the batch recorded: parameter inference does not descend two deep and reads data that A11/A12 lose (stage 2); a type-valued filler that is a Π-type or `D(ā)` is excluded from the substitution pair (stage 3). The run's side of every row is still read off the clause except where a kernel-checked lemma is named; a kernel-checked run of one accepted program per form remains the open check (it needs a concrete data model and decisions under `run`, which is noncomputable: by `simp` lemmas per clause, as `Attack/` does for [Close]).

**Plan.** One definitions batch for A1–A10 (the join's entry set, the proof skip, the observation, access by observation, D67's owned access, structure through annotations, parameters, results of `fix` and captures, parameter inference in `runFields`), then one repair of the four core lemmas. A11, A12 (structure of a call's result and of a field: `PiOut` and the data model's field types carry a `TyM` only) and A13 (open values: `Sem`, `ctorM` and `Membership` together) are not in the batch; each is its own redesign and stays a row here until done.

### D20. What the step skeleton still lacks to compose (second lead, 2026-11-13; open, ranked)

Found while trying to derive `nfSealed_step` from the hypothesis at `fuel`. No step proof exists yet, so none of this was visible. Each item is a fault of the statements (not of the checker), with its fix. They are read off the statements and the checker's code; none is kernel-checked, and each should be before its fix is taken as settled:

1. **Sequencing. DECIDED: the simulation form; the old form is KERNEL-CHECKED FALSE; relation and central statement in Lean, the 72 statements not yet migrated.** A step was stated from `SM' K ρ st`, the checker's own state read as a reference state, and concluded `Below (mean st') s'.toMean`. Two faults, both of the statements:
   - *It does not compose.* After a first step the reference is in its own state `s'`, not in `SM' st'`; the second step's statement says nothing of a run from `s'`.
   - *It is false wherever a borrow was held* (`Regress/StaleTemp.lean`). `Below` reads every position, temporaries included. The reference parks a borrow in a temporary of the top frame until it is bound, and when it is bound the temporary stays, reading `⊥`; the checker carries a value in flight in its monad and has no temporary there; a missing position means `err`, and `err ⊑ ⊥` is false. On the accepted program `let r = &y; ()`: the checker (typed) ends with one binding and no temporary (`checkerNoTemps`, a `#guard`); the reference run ends in a state whose temporary `0` reads `⊥` (`let_borrow_run`, `aF_temp`, kernel-checked); no state with no temporary there is `Below` it (`not_below_stale_temp`, kernel-checked). So `letIn_step_stmt`, `eval_step_stmt` and `evalCore_step_stmt` were false on the simplest program that holds a borrow. (The pilot's `withTemp` aligns the one value in flight at a read or a borrow; it does not survive the value being bound.)

   **The alternative considered and rejected: one lemma "the run is monotone in the state".** It is false for `Below`: two states can be `Below`-related by a witness future that no [End] would fix (the lower reads `5` where the upper's holder holds `7`; a read then gives `5` against `7`), and `Below` relates meanings only, so two states with equal meanings and different binding counts resolve `var 0` differently. For a relation generated by early ends it would still need the run from the lower state to FAIL when it uses a holder the lower state has forgotten, and a write through `*r` does not fail (`Sem.updAt` at a `deref` of `⊥` succeeds). In the simulation form the checker's own run is a hypothesis, and the checker never uses a holder it ended, so that case does not arise.

   **The simulation form** (`Steps/Sim.lean`). A step is stated from ANY reference state `s` with `Rel K ρ φ st s`, and concludes `Rel` of the two states after. `Rel` has three parts, each forced by a counterexample above:
   - `skel`: the same frames, each with the same bindings at the same type meaning, data and declared reading.
   - `fresh`, with a renaming `π` of the checker's labels to the reference's, carried as a parameter of `Rel` and extended by each step. The two runs do NOT issue the same labels and cannot be made to: the checker runs an erased term on a private copy and keeps the label counter (D37), so a proof that borrows (`lemma(&x)`) consumes labels; the reference does not run a proof at all (the audit's A3), and its run of one could fail. The first version of `Rel` asked for equal counters and was false after any such proof. So shapes are compared through `π`, the checker's state and value are read at the run's future pulled back along `π`, and `fresh` says every label the checker has issued is renamed below the reference's counter, so the next labels are new on both sides.
   - `early`: for some list `E` of labels, the checker's state is the reference's after the borrows of `E` ended, compared at the SAME future (`SimEq`): bindings position by position; a borrow in flight in the checker's state held, by LABEL, by some temporary of the same frame whose content is above it; temporaries otherwise not compared. The futures compared are those that agree with the run's off the labels live after the ends, which is item 2's repair, folded in.

   Proved (kernel-checked, standard axioms): `Rel.start` (the relation holds at the start, `Rel K ρ φ id st (SM' K ρ st)`), `sim_seq` (two steps in the simulation form compose; with the old form the second premise exists only at `SM' st₁`), `Sim.of_below` and `Sim.of_simEq_below` (the new order is implied by the old where the old holds), and `StaleTemp.sim_stale_temp` (the new order HOLDS on the instance where `Below` fails).

   **Migrated to the simulation form:** the three refuted statements, `evalCore_step_stmt`, `eval_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` (`Statements.lean`; their area theorems are `sorry` as before, so nothing was re-proved). **Not yet:** every other statement with a `Below (MState.mean … st') s'.toMean` conjunct against a run from `SM'`: the leaves (read, borrow, assign, access, drop, pop), matches, calls, `J`, [Close], the tail forms. `Statements.lean`'s header says which. On the way, `hns` of `evalCore_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` and `J_step_stmt` was strengthened from "not a statement" to "neither a statement nor erased": since the audit's A3 the run skips an erased term, which `evalCore` runs, so the old side condition left them false at an erased term.

   **What the migration needs first, in order of risk:**
   - (a) **The renaming on a label that survives a private copy.** `Rel`'s renaming absorbs the audit's A15 (the reference restores the counter after a copy and reuses the labels it issued inside; the checker does not): those labels are dead on both sides. That is so only if no label the checker issues inside a private copy is still in its state after the copy. D37 keeps the counter because some records survive a copy; whether a LOAN can is not checked. If one can, it has no counterpart in the reference.
   - (b) **Shape agreement.** The skeleton leaves out a binding's Π-shape: the checker's binding reads it off its stored type (`Value.tyS`, at the constant future, in the frame of the type's captures), the reference's binding has the shape its run made (`runBinds` in the current state). They are the same only if a Π-type's shape does not depend on the state or the future it is read in. A call step needs it. Believed true (types are hole-free and closed over their captures); unstated.
   - (c) **`Rel` at the start. PROVED:** `Rel.start : Rel K ρ φ id st (SM' K ρ st)`, with no renaming and no early end.
   - (d) **`Rel` under an extended assignment**: `eval_sim_stmt` takes `Rel` at `ρ` and runs at `ρ'`. Item 7's locality (`run_abs_congr`) is what carries it.
   - (e) **Each leaf step from a related state**, not from `SM' st`: [Read], [Borrow], [Assign], the tag of a match, a call. This is where "an early end is harmless" is now proved, once per leaf, with the checker's run in hand: the leaf reads a place the checker has not forgotten, so the reference's access ends, among the labels of `E`, exactly those the place depends on, and what it then reads is what the checker read. The riskiest is the join's drop set (`movedPlaces` reads which roots are `⊥`, and a forgotten holder is `⊥` in the checker's state and not in the reference's) and `Id` (its observation reads every position the side started with, temporaries included).

2. **`Below` reads the reference state at futures other than the run's.** The run at `φ` stores constants read at `φ` (`let x = loan_ℓ` binds `fun _ => φ ℓ`), while the checker's `x ↦ loan_ℓ` means `φ' ℓ` at `φ'`. So `Below (mean st') s'.toMean` is false whenever a binding holds an inert loan, which is every sealed program's re-run with a hole. The order should range over futures that agree with the run's off the reference state's live labels.
3. **Inert loans in a nested run.** A sealed program's re-run binds its holes (`loan_ℓ` whose borrow is outside the run) in its private environment, where (WF2) and `TypedFuture` know nothing of `ℓ`. So the nested states are not `Good` and the hypothesis at `fuel` does not apply to them. `Good` needs the free labels of a nested run typed, as `GoodF` types the labels in flight.
4. **Untyped runs.** `evalCore_step_stmt` concludes "the reference run does not fail" for every term, typed or not. For `typed = false` on arbitrary text it is false (an unannotated `let x = Nil` succeeds in the checker's untyped run with a bare constructor and has no meaning). An untyped run is of checked code only (a callee's body, a sealed program's text); the statement needs that as a hypothesis, in the form the proof can use: the reference run of the code from the state above does not fail (membership of the callee's meaning gives it for a body, `Membership` and the substitution lemma for a re-run).
5. **`eval` is not `evalCore`. STATED.** `Machine.eval` is `evalAt`, which classifies the term, runs an erased one on a copy and restores `env`; the skeleton stated only `evalCore`, and every recursive position of the checker calls `eval`. Now `eval_step_stmt` is in the skeleton (statement, IH field, knot, area theorem `sorry`): `eval` against the run, with no side condition, since the run erases at `Decisions.flags` as `evalAt` does at `preFlags`. `evalCore_step_stmt`, `letIn_step_stmt` and `J_step_stmt` gain `hns` (the term is not a statement): since D19 the run of a statement is on a copy and `evalCore` does not erase, so without it they would be false. `proof_step_stmt` is stated for `eval`: for `evalCore` its "the state is unchanged" was false of a proof-valued `let` whose right-hand side moves a place (read, not kernel-checked).
6. **Free parameters of every statement. KERNEL-CHECKED AND FIXED** (but for `K.D`). Nothing tied `K.Γ` to `st.inds` (a constructor of a declared type has no meaning at `K.Γ = []`), `K.dm` to covering `K.Γ`, or `K.C.global` to `st.globals`: `.const n` means `K.C.global n`, so `evalCore_step_stmt K ρ φ 10` is false at every `K` whose `K.C.global "one"` is not Σ's (`Regress/ConstFree_70247f07.lean`, unbuilt record; the kernel runs the checker's `evalCore` on the global). Fixed: `CtxOk K ρ st` replaces `DeltaOk` as the hypothesis of all 72 statements; it holds the state's declarations, a data model covering them, `DeltaOk`, label-free globals (`run_congr`'s `hK`) and `GlobalsOk K st`: each global has its stored type, a constant means its stored value at it, a function means the closure of its `fix` term with its shape, and each is a member of its type (the outer induction of docs/18's theorem, now a named hypothesis). `Regress/ConstFree_flipped.lean` (built) is the positive form. Still free: `K.D` outside the `*_aligned` statements, and `K.Λ`.
7. **The run context changes with the state.** `K.Δ` is `deltaOf` of the state's unknowns, so a step that creates unknowns must hand on an extended context with its extended assignment (`∃ ρ' K'`), and what was established under `K` must carry to `K'`: the run reads the assignment and `Δ` only at the unknowns that occur in its term. That locality lemma, `Lemmas.run_abs_congr`, is PROVED (2026-11-21, from `AssignCongr.run_congrA`; standard axioms). Still to do: no statement extends `K` yet (the eight with `∃ ρ'` conclude under the `K` they were given).
8. **Unknowns of a discarded world. KERNEL-CHECKED AND FIXED.** `exitWorld` keeps the types of the unknowns an arm created (D37), and `TypedAssignment.mem` asked `ρ σ ∈ ⟦Δ(σ)⟧` of every `σ < absTy.size`. An arm's field unknown can have an empty type in the world the assignment selects, so no assignment of the state after such a split would be typed; and `exitWorld_step_stmt` was false outright, since it concludes `Good ρ st'` at the same `ρ`, which is unconstrained at the arm's unknowns (`Regress/ExitWorld_f32a7048.lean`: `¬ exitWorld_step_stmt K (fun _ => err) φ fuel` at every `K`, `φ`, `fuel`; unbuilt record). Fixed: `MState.Refd st σ` (σ occurs in a value of Ω, a stored or deferred type, the goal, a record in force, or the type of a referenced unknown), and `TypedAssignment.mem` and `Membership.absTy` range over referenced unknowns, as docs/18's "each abstract value σ of Ω" says. `Regress/ExitWorld_flipped.lean` (built): the state of the record is `Good` at the assignment that refuted it.
9. **`st'.core = st.core` after a re-run.** `nfSealed_step_stmt` (and `substV_*`) claim the core unchanged; `restoreKeep` keeps the unknowns and the η and generalisation records a nested run made, and an untyped run can η-refine at a join (`movedPlace`). Whether a re-run does so on reachable states is not settled; the statement should hand on an extended assignment instead.
10. **The renaming lemma** (`run_counter_indep`, above), needed by 5's sealed and body cases.

The ranked list above ("Risk list, ranked") is superseded in its order by: D19's R1–R5 (the run is wrong on common programs), then 1–4 here (nothing composes without them), then 5–10, then its items 5–8.

### Milestone 2: the reference run (`Run.lean`, the meaning of code [Q2]) — in progress

Done (all building, structurally recursive, no `partial`):
- `Pi.lean`: per-level carriers `el0/el1/el2` with encodings; `PiParam`/`PiOut`; `piCar0` (telescope with final contents of borrow parameters; output data × finals or `Back` with an injective backward function), `piProp` (impredicative), `piCar1`/`piCar2`. Three `rfl` examples against `Slice.lean`'s shapes.
- `SState.lean`: the run's state, a Value-free `Env` (bindings with type, meaning as a function of the future, declared reading, and the guarded-recursion bound for a function's binding of itself; temporaries; shape; label counter); `endLabel` (lifted `semEnd`), `access` (ends, iterated in label order, exactly the labels the position's meaning depends on), `MState.sstate`.
- `Apply.lean`: `dec0`/`dec1`; `applyTele` (apply a Π-carrier element along its telescope); `mkTele` (build one from a body's run at every argument, `none` where the body is undefined or ill-typed, or the backward function is not injective: such closures mean `err`). `BodyOut` is what a body's run yields: result and finals as functions of the future, and the returned label.
- `Places.lean`: `Decisions`, the interface to `Decide.lean` (flags, placeDecl, declOf, copy, piSort, joinMoves): the *aligned* decisions the run must make exactly as the checker does; the step lemmas prove the fields equal the checker's functions. `Sem.step/follow/updAt` on meanings at a type (`*` is the identity; fields through `DataModel.paramsOf`/`fieldsOf`/`fieldTy`; re-packing through `ctorM`).
- `Run.lean`: `SM`, `RunCtx`, results `SVal × TyM` (a result carries its type, since projections and hints need it), `readPlace` (access, copy or move by the declared reading), `borrowPlace`, `setContent`, and `run` for place, borrow, assign, let (binding at the rhs's type; drop = forget), seq, const, val, sort, nat/unit/zero/tt/succ, eq (both sides on copies; equality in `Sem`), the proof primitives (`⋆`), ref, clone/peek/inplace, ascribe.

**D8 revised (2026-11-09).** data-generic refuted the carrier-by-choice reading: a carrier does not determine the parameters (`Pair(True, B)` and `Pair(PLift True, B)` have one carrier `PLift True × El B`, and packing `[⋆, b]` succeeds at one and fails at the other), so `pack_congr` is unprovable and `paramsOf` could pick parameters at which the actual fields do not pack. The hint is now *structured*: `TyS` (in `Pi.lean`) gained `data : Option (String × List Sem)`, the declaration's name and its parameters' meanings, and a `ps = []` constructor value reads its parameters straight off `hint.data` (no choice). `Value.tyS` builds the hint of a stored type value; Nested fields and parameters get structured hints from `DataModel.fieldData`/`paramData` (data-generic, landed ccc9510a), so nested `ps = []` values are covered; only fields reached by projection (`Sem.follow`) still lack data. `paramsOf` stays only as an unused fallback. `rank_lt` is likewise restated relative to declared recursive positions (`recArgs`, data-generic), since carrier equality cannot order ranks (`Nat = Nat × Unit` is consistent).

### D9. The run's types carry the Π-shape (`Run.lean`, `TyS`)

Applying a function value needs its Π-shape (`PiParam`s and `PiOut`): neither the opaque carrier `ty0 (piCar0 …)` nor the value can give it back, and recomputing it from the callee's Π-type *value* means running that value's domain terms, which are not subterms of the call, so `run` could not stay structurally recursive. Storing the shape in `Sem` is impossible (`PiParam.dom : List Sem → TyM` would put `Sem` in a negative position). So the run computes *structured* types: `TyS := (m : TyM, shape : Option (List PiParam × PiOut))`, and every result, binding and (through `CodeMeaning.piShape`, to add) stored Π-type carries its shape. A call evaluates its arguments at the parameter types from the callee's shape (`runArgs`, D82's [Call-type]) and hands `callHole` evaluated pieces. `run` also takes the type its context expects (`hint`, D8): a `let` binds at its right-hand side's type, an assignment runs its value at the place's type, fields at their field types (`runFields`), parameters at theirs (`runParams`), `Eq`'s sides at `A`.

Filled so far: constructor values (`ctorOf` + the `.ctor` clause, parameters read off the hint when unwritten) and `D(ā)`; Π-type formation (`.pi` clause with `runDoms`: each domain term run in the forming state extended by the earlier parameters' meanings, the output likewise, the carrier chosen by `Decisions.piSort`: `piProp`/`piCar0`/`piCar1`/`piCar2`, and the shape kept in `TyS.shape`); calls (`callOf` on the evaluated callee and arguments: `applyTele` along the shape; a data result fixes each lent place's future at the argument's label (`TyS.label`) to its final content; a borrow result takes a fresh label `k` and each lent place reads its component of the backward function at `k`'s final content; a statement call runs on a copy, by `Decisions.flags`).

Known gaps in what is filled, for milestone 3 to state or for a later pass: (i) a recursive call's guard (`SBinding.recBound`) is not yet checked in the `.call` clause (it belongs with `fixHole`, which sets the bound); (ii) a call whose result is itself a function gets no shape (`PiOut` carries only the output's `TyM`; extend `PiOut.data` with an optional shape when a higher-order-returning example needs it); (iii) `runDoms` binds the earlier parameters with type `err` and no declared reading, so a domain term that reads a parameter's declared type (copy-or-move inside a type-level match) reads `other`.

Also filled: matches (`.matchNat`/`.matchInd`: the arm by the tag of the accessed scrutinee's meaning, a proof scrutinee by its type's one constructor (D45), then D84's `joinDrop` setting every place in `Decisions.joinMoves` to `moved`; `runArm` keeps the recursion structural); `J` (the transported term, the other arguments statements on copies); `Id` (`observation`: each side run on a copy at `A`, every live borrow of the copy ended, the result and every position compared, all under the constant future). Known gap: the match's access uses read mode where the checker's uses match mode (ends only loans at a neutral head, D29); the reference ends only what the tag depends on, which is at most that, so the step lemma is unaffected, but `contentOf` should take a mode when the step lemmas are written.

Closure formation (`.fix` clause) is filled: the closure means `el0 (piCar0 …) (mkTele … g)` where `g` runs the body in the forming frame extended by `self` and the parameters (the body's layout `[caps…, self, x̄]`, captures being the forming frame's bindings read in place), ends the borrows the body made (labels ≥ the label counter at formation: the frame pop) and reads each borrow parameter's holder for its final content; forming moves the captured places whose readings are not copy (D80). A recursive `fix` (`by xⱼ`) is its guarded fixpoint **by stages** rather than by `WellFounded.fix`: stage 0 binds `self` to `err`, stage `k+1` to stage `k`'s closure, and the function at arguments whose decreasing argument has rank `r` (`DataModel.rank` through its carrier; a `Nat`'s value) is stage `r+1`. A chain of calls that decreases by at least one in rank per call never reaches stage 0; a call that does not decrease does, means `err`, and `mkTele` then makes the whole closure mean `err`: [Rec]'s accepting direction is exactly what makes the guard never fire (docs/24 §4). The lemma that stages stabilise for a decreasing body (the semantic form of [Rec], `Slice.lean`'s `stage_stable` generalised) is milestone 3's.

Known gaps of the `.fix` clause: (i) a borrow parameter whose holder the body moved (passed on whole) reads `moved` as its final content where the checker's pop would end the borrow wherever its holder went; (ii) the parameter bindings carry no declared readings (`SBinding.decl`), as in `runDoms`; (iii) `self`'s binding is not marked with `recBound`, since the guard is realised by the stages instead.

**The knot** (`Run.lean`, after the mutual block). `meanV K ρ φ hint : Value → Sem` is `Value.mean` with the code-dependent forms given by the run itself: a sealed program is its program run from `emptyState` at the hint, read under the future; a closure or Π-closure is its `fix`/`pi` term run in a frame of its captures at their recorded types (`capsVals`, `capsTys`, `capsState`; D51/D82); a global is `CodeMeaning.global`. `run`'s `.val` clause uses `meanV`, so the whole thing is one structural recursion over `Term`/`Value`. `codeMeaning K ρ : CodeMeaning` packages `meanV` for `Value.mean`; its assignment argument is ignored (a state's meaning is only read at the state's own assignment), and `paramTy` reads the shape of the Π-type value through `run`. `meanV_eq_mean` (stated, `sorry`): `Value.mean K.dm (codeMeaning K ρ) K.Γ ρ φ hint v = meanV K ρ φ hint v`, by induction on `v`; every clause but the code-dependent ones is the same text.

**Pure substitution** (`Subst.lean`): `Value.substLoan`/`Term.substLoan`, replacing `loan ℓ` by a value everywhere (inside sealed programs, closures and types), the pure half of the checker's `substV`; re-normalisation is the run's own clause.

**Statements closing milestone 2** (`Lemmas.lean`, `sorry`): `meanV_substLoan` and `run_substLoan` (filling a loan is fixing the future, for values and for a term's run from a state; hypotheses: `w` does not mention `ℓ`, and `w`'s meaning is hint-free), `meanV_congr` (a meaning reads the future only at the value's loans). Still to write for milestone 2: the generic `DataModel` by stages (D4) and the program meaning (globals by recursion on the program, instantiating `CodeMeaning.global`), and `Decisions` instantiated by the checker's `Decide` functions (the latter is where "aligned" is discharged, so it may be milestone 3's).

### Milestone 3: the step-lemma skeleton (`Steps/*.lean`) — started

`Steps/Borrow.lean` fixes the shape: `Runs f st fuel a st'` (the checker's `runMachine` succeeds), `Good ρ st` as the only hypothesis on the state, the conclusion `Below (MState.mean … st') (semantic step on (MState.sstate … st))` with `Exact` where the step is exact, and `Good ρ st'`; the semantic side is `SState`'s primitive (`access`, `readPlace`, `borrowPlace`, `setContent`, `dropLast`); the checker's functions are referred to as `Ochr.readPlace` etc., since the run's shadow them. Statements: `endBorrow_step` (exact = `semEnd`), `accessPath_step`/`accessInside_step` (below `access`), `readPlace_step`, `borrowPlace_step` (exact against the run's, with the copy decision aligned through `Decisions.copy`), `assignPlace_step`, `dropTopBind_step`, `popFrame_step`.

One file per checker area, one lemma per rule, each of the form "if `runMachine (f …) st fuel = ok a st'` and `Good ρ st`, then for every typed future the result and `st'` are below the reference step on `st.mean ρ`, and `Good ρ' st'` for the extended assignment". Rough partition and counts, by `Machine.lean`'s sections: `Steps/Borrow.lean` (End, Access ×3, Read, Copy, Borrow, Assign, Drop ×3, popFrame: 12), `Steps/Subst.lean` (substV, substEnv, nfSealed, canonNeutral, renormAll, refine: 6), `Steps/Call.lean` (callFn, runBody, closeCall ×3 rows, callType/declCallType, paramTypes, recCheck's accepting direction: 9), `Steps/Match.lean` (evalMatch, evalMatchInd, evalMatchByType, splitThenClose, generalizeNeutral, blockCaptures, closeBlock, enter/exitWorld: 8), `Steps/Eq.lean` (mkEqM, conv, convPi, convFn, rewriteGoal, cong/symm/trans: 7), `Steps/Obs.lean` (observe, idType, footprint coverage, owners: 4), `Steps/Types.lean` (evalType, piSort, capture, evalTInd, evalCtor, fieldTypes, repackCheck, lentUnknown, valType? soundness: 9), `Steps/Eval.lean` (evalCore's remaining forms, letIn, seq, ascribe, prim: 8), `Steps/Tail.lean` (checkTail, checkFix, checkDef, checkInd, Ind-decl conditions (I1)–(I9): 6), `Steps/WF.lean` (preservation of each WF clause per step family: 7). About 75 statements, each owned by the spine; provers own private auxiliaries. The five supporting lemmas of docs/26 (membership, `valType?` soundness, footprint coverage, conversion soundness, WF preservation) are spine statements in `Types`, `Obs`, `Eq` and `WF`.

### Milestone 4: assembly (`Consistency.lean` under `Ochr/Meaning`)

`consistencyBounded` from: the program meaning (globals by recursion on the program), Corollary "an accepted definition means an element of its type" from the step lemmas by induction on `checkTail`'s case tree, conversion soundness at the leaf, and `⟦False⟧ = prop False`. `#print axioms` must show `sorryAx` plus the standard three. Then sketches for Target B (error reflection and the borrow-structure lemma against `Model/Safety.lean`'s shape; realizability [Q4] stated, not proved) and Target C (the context lemma stated).

## HANDOVER (written 2026-11-09 at 8736099f, for a lead picking this up cold)

### Architecture, in one pass

`Ochr/Meaning/` is a lean_lib `OchrMeaning` importing the checker (`Ochr.Env`, `Ochr.Obs`, `Ochr.Machine`) and defining, in dependency order:

1. `Sem.lean`: `TyM` (type meanings: `prop P | ty0 A | ty1 A | ty2 A | univ l | err`) and `Sem` (value meanings tagged by carrier: `el0/el1/el2 A a | ty T | proof | moved | ctor name c fs | err`), membership `∈ₛ` (tag = carrier), the order `⊑` (`moved` below all, `ctor` componentwise, else equality; `le_eq_of_whole`, `le_trans` proved), `pmem` (partly moved membership). Invariant: a *whole* constructor value is always `el0` of its carrier; `ctor` appears only with a `moved` somewhere inside (`DataModel.ctorM` enforces it).
2. `Data.lean`: `DataModel` (carrier per declaration and parameter meanings; `pack/tag/fields/rank`; `fieldTy`/`paramTy` the telescope's field and parameter types; the lfp laws), `tyM` (`ty0 car`, or `prop (Nonempty car)` for `Prop`-sorted), `ctorM`, `fieldsOf`, `paramsOf` (D8: parameters recovered from a carrier by choice, `paramsOf_car`), `libModel` (Pair/False/True/And). Owned by implementer data-generic while it adds `pack_congr` and `DataGeneric.lean`.
3. `Future.lean`: `Assignment := Nat → Sem`, `Future := Nat → Sem`, `Future.upd`.
4. `Value.lean`: `CodeMeaning` (global, sealed, clo, pi, paramTy: the code-dependent meanings, a parameter), `Value.mean dm C Γ ρ φ hint : Value → Sem` with `meanFields`/`meanParams` threading hints. Invariant: `hint` is read only by `.ind` with `ps = []`; `.borrow _ w` means `w` (current content); `.loan l` means `φ l` (final content); `.tRef A` means `A`'s meaning.
5. `State.lean`: `Env.shape` (which position holds the borrow of which label; WF1 makes it a map), `Env.hintAt` (a position's stored type as TyM, or `err`), `Env.mean`, `Mean` (positions, `val : Future → Pos → Sem`, `shape`), `MState.mean`, `MState.Core`/`mean_core` (the relevance table is the module docstring), `posTyM`, `absTyM`, `goalM`, `Below`, `Mean.semEnd` (proved below), `Mean.forget`, `dependsOn`.
6. `WF.lean`: WF1–WF4, WF6 (naming/freshness), WF7, WF8 (`&` only at the top of a type), `ArgsLoanFree` (WF5, a call-site property), `TypedFuture`, `TypedAssignment` (membership of `ρ σ` in `⟦Δ σ⟧`, path conditions for `neutrals` and `etaRefs`), `Membership`, `Good := TypedAssignment ∧ Membership ∧ WF` (the main theorem's hypothesis).
7. `Pi.lean`: `el0/el1/el2` carriers per level with `enc0/1/2`; `PiParam (isRef, dom : List Sem → TyM)`, `PiOut (data B | ref U)`, `Back`, `piCar0` (level-0 Π carrier: telescope over parameters accumulating the prefix of meanings and the `Fin` type of final contents of borrow parameters; output `B × Fin` or `Back Fin U`), `piProp`, `piCar1/2`.
8. `SState.lean`: the run's state (`SBinding (ty, val : SVal, decl, recBound)`, frames with temps, `shape`, `nextLoan`), `valAt/tyAt/setAt/positions/toMean/mapVals/endLabel/liveLabels/access/varPos/push*/freshLoan/dropLast`, `MState.sstate` (a checker state read into one, via `hintAt`/`mean`).
9. `Apply.lean`: `dec0/dec1` (decode at a carrier, classical), `extFin/extRead/extMk`, `AppResult`, `applyTele`, `BodyOut (result, finals : List SVal, retLabel)`, `noFuture`, `mkLeafData/mkBackAt/mkLeafRef`, `mkTele`. Owned by m2-proofs for `dec0_enc0` only.
10. `Places.lean`: `Decisions` (flags, placeDecl, declOf, copy, piSort, joinMoves: the aligned decisions read from syntax; to be instantiated by `Decide.lean` in `DecisionsInst.lean`, implementer prog-decisions), `TyM.dataOf`, `Sem.stepField/step/follow/updAt` (places on meanings at a type).
11. `Run.lean`: `SM` (state transformer with failure), `RunCtx (dm, Γ, C, D)`, `TyS (m, shape, label)` (D9), `Res := SVal × TyS`, `scopeOf`, `extendBy`, `emptyState`, `capsState/stateOf`, `resolve`, `contentOf` (access + follow, future-free content), `setContent`, `readPlace`, `borrowPlace`, `Future.updList`, `callOf`, `joinDrop`, `observation`; the mutual block `run`/`runArm`/`runDoms`/`runParams`/`runFields`/`runArgs`/`meanV`/`meanFields`/`meanParams`/`capsVals`/`capsTys`; then `codeMeaning K ρ` and `meanV_eq_mean` (sorry; owned by m2-proofs).
12. `Subst.lean` (pure `substLoan`), `Lemmas.lean` (`HintFree`, `meanV_substLoan`, `run_substLoan`, `meanV_congr`; owned by m2-proofs), `Bounded.lean` (Q1), `Sanity.lean`, `Steps/*.lean` (milestone 3).

How they fit: a checker state `st` and an assignment `ρ` give `MState.mean` (for part (a)) and `MState.sstate` (the run's input). The reference run `run K ρ hint t : SM Res` transforms `SState`s; a state is read through `K.M = codeMeaning K`, so `.val`/sealed/closures mean what the run says (`meanV_eq_mean`; D18: no knot hypothesis), and `K.D` the checker's `Decide` functions for the aligned decisions. A step lemma (`Steps/Borrow.lean` fixes the shape) says: `Runs f st fuel a st'` and `Good ρ st` imply `Below (MState.mean st') (semantic step on (MState.sstate st)).toMean` (or `Exact`) and `Good ρ st'`.

### Conventions an implementer must keep

- **Totality and `err`**: every semantic function is total; an ill-formed input means `Sem.err`/`TyM.err`/`none` (the run fails with `none`). Never add a `partial` or an axiom; everything is `noncomputable` and uses `Classical` freely (`open Classical` in Apply/Places/Run).
- **Futures**: a position's meaning is `SVal := Future → Sem`; read a value that is future-free under `noFuture`; `Future.upd`/`updList` fix labels; ending a label is `endLabel` (the holder forgotten, every meaning reads the label as the holder's current content). A fresh label comes from `freshLoan`; a borrow result carries its label in `TyS.label` so a call can fix its lender's future.
- **Hints (D8)**: `run hint t` and `meanV φ hint v` read a value at the type its context expects; pass `.err` when there is none; only `ps = []` constructor values use it. Field hints come from `fieldTy`, parameter hints from `paramTy`, argument hints from the callee's shape.
- **Shapes (D9)**: a Π-type's shape travels in `TyS.shape`; a function value's result carries none (gap (ii) below).
- **Statements** run on a copy: `SM.onCopy`. Which terms are statements is `Decisions.flags`, never a check of the value.
- **Open recursion**: everything that runs code must be in `Run.lean`'s mutual block, structurally recursive on `Term`/`Value`; a call to `run` on a lambda-bound term (e.g. inside `List.map`) is rejected by the termination checker: write a mutual list helper (`runDoms`, `runArm`, `runFields`, …) instead. `Nat.rec` with a `run body` call inside its step is accepted (the `fix` clause's stages).
- **Checker names shadowed**: the run's `readPlace`/`borrowPlace` shadow `Ochr.readPlace`/`Ochr.borrowPlace`; qualify the checker's in statements.
- **No Mathlib**: `List.Forall₂` etc. are unavailable; the project imports Lean core only.

### The `fix` clause (filled) and its spec

A closure `fix self (x̄ : Ā) : B by xⱼ := body` formed in state `s` means `el0 (piCar0 ps [] PUnit out) F` (or `err`), where `ps := runDoms s ds` (each domain term run in `s` extended by the earlier parameters; `&A` marks a borrow parameter with content type `A`), `out` is the codomain term's meaning per prefix (`.ref U` for a borrow result), and `F := mkTele ps [] PUnit (fun fs => some ((), fs)) out g`. `g args` runs `body` in `s` extended by `self` (type `ty0 piT`, value `selfM`) and the parameters (each at its `dom` of the prefix; a borrow parameter's holder's value is the argument's content), at the codomain hint; then ends every label ≥ the label counter at formation (the frame pop ending the body's borrows) and reads each borrow parameter's holder for its final content; `retLabel` is the result's `TyS.label`. Non-recursive: `g := bodyAt .err`. Recursive: `g args := stage (rankOf args + 1) args` with `stage 0 := fun _ => none`, `stage (k+1) := bodyAt (closureOf (stage k))`, and `rankOf` the `DataModel.rank` of argument `j` through the carrier of its parameter type (a `Nat`'s value). Forming moves non-copy captured places (`Decisions.copy` of their reading). **Not done**: `SBinding.recBound` is unused (the guard is realised by the stages); a `WellFounded.fix` formulation was dropped because the carrier holds total functions only and a "restricted" element cannot be built. The stabilisation lemma for a decreasing body (stage `k > rank` agrees with stage `rank + 1`) is the semantic form of [Rec] and is a milestone-3 statement (`Steps/Call.lean`, `recCheck`'s accepting direction).

### The knot and what could go wrong

`codeMeaning K` packages `meanV K` as a `CodeMeaning`, reading at the assignment and future it is passed (D18; as first written it ignored them, a definition fault); `meanV_eq_mean` says `Value.mean K.dm (codeMeaning K) K.Γ ρ φ hint v = meanV K ρ φ hint v`. It is well founded because `meanV` is defined *inside* the mutual block by structural recursion (a sealed program's run is on the term inside the value; a closure's on the term inside the value), so `codeMeaning` is a plain definition after the block, not a fixpoint. Risks: (1) `K.C.global` must be the program meaning (`Program.lean`, implementer prog-decisions) and is the one truly external input; (2) `meanV`'s `.tPi`/`.clo` clauses run the term in `stateOf (capsState (capsVals φ caps) (capsTys φ rd))`, which has no declared readings beyond `rd`'s and no label counter, so a closure whose code reads a capture's declared type for copy-or-move must find it in `rd` (D82 says it does); (3) `codeMeaning.paramTy` reads the Π-type value's shape through `run … (.val P) emptyState`, which goes through `meanV (.tPi …)` and loses the shape (a `.val` result has `shape := none`): **this is wrong as written** and must be fixed by computing the shape from the `tPi`'s term as the `.pi` clause does (make a `piShapeOf` helper in the block returning `Option (List PiParam × PiOut)` for a `tPi caps (pi hs ds c) rd`, and use it both in `.pi`'s result and here). The same helper is what `Steps/Call.lean` needs to state the callee's shape from its type value.

### Statements to write or fill (as I would write them)

- `meanV_substLoan`, `run_substLoan`, `meanV_congr`: in `Lemmas.lean` as stated. The proof of `run_substLoan` is by the mutual induction with `meanV_substLoan`; the hypothesis `HintFree` can be dropped if `w` is a hole-free whole value whose meaning ignores the hint (every value but a `ps = []` constructor).
- Generic `DataModel` (data-generic): `stage : IndDecl → Nat → List Sem → Type`, `stage d 0 ps := PEmpty`, `stage d (k+1) ps := Σ c : Fin ctors, Tele d c ps (fun ps' => stage d k ps')` where `Tele` is the Σ-telescope of field carriers read from `fieldTy` with recursive occurrences `D(ps')` at stage `k`; carrier `car d ps := Quot (Σ k, stage d k ps) (embedding)`; laws by induction on stages; `pack_congr : car d ps = car d ps' → pack d ps c fs ≅ pack d ps' c fs` (HEq). `fieldTy` is the reference run of the declaration's field type terms at the parameters and earlier fields (so `DataGeneric.lean` must either take it as a parameter or be defined after `Run.lean`; parameter is simpler, instantiated in `Program.lean`).
- Program meaning (prog-decisions): `progMeaning : List Item → (String → Sem)`, by recursion on the program, each definition's meaning `meanV` of its `fix` term (or its constant's value) under the previous globals, in `emptyState`, at `ρ := fun _ => .err` (a definition is closed); `inds` from the program's inductive declarations.
- `Decisions` instance (prog-decisions): each field from `Decide.lean` run with `runMachine` on a state holding `inds`/`globals`/`constDecls` (these functions read only syntax and declarations; take the `Option`-defaulted result where the monadic function can fail).

### Known gaps (all recorded above; collected)

(i) recursive-call guard realised by stages only, `recBound` unused; (ii) a call returning a function gets no shape (extend `PiOut.data` with an optional shape); (iii) `runDoms`/`fix` bind parameters without declared readings (`decl := .other`), so a type-level `match` reading a parameter's copy-ness reads `other`; (iv) match access uses read mode, not match mode (reference ends at most what the checker does; harmless for the step lemma); (v) a borrow parameter whose holder the body moved reads `moved` as its final content; (vi) `codeMeaning.paramTy` loses the shape (see the knot); (vii) `capsState` frames have `nextLoan := 0`, so labels created inside a sealed program's re-run could collide with the outer state's in the *statement* of a step lemma comparing them (the run's labels are its own; a step lemma should compare up to the fresh labels it creates); (viii) `MState.sstate` sets no `recBound`; (ix) `Env.hintAt` reads `lazyTy` through `CodeMeaning.paramTy` (fixed with piShapeOf in 059d7906); (x) closed: closures at every level through `Apply1.lean` (D12); residual: `Prop`/`Type₀` as elements of `Type₁` do not decode at level 2; (xi) `movedPlaces` lists whole top-frame variables, where d84's `botPlaces` lists sub-places too.

### Pitfalls met, and choices I would revisit

- Lean namespaces: a `def Value.foo` inside `namespace Ochr.Meaning` is `Ochr.Meaning.Value.foo`, and dot notation `v.foo` looks for `Ochr.Value.foo`; use `_root_.Ochr.Value.foo`. Dot notation also lands the receiver on the *first* explicit argument of that type (`v.substLoan l w` put `v` in `w`'s slot).
- `at` and `rec` are reserved names for fields.
- Docstrings must sit inside `mutual` blocks, before each declaration, not before `mutual`.
- `do`-destructuring `let (a, b) ← …` at `Type 3` confused universe inference; `match` instead.
- Section variables are shared by every function of a mutual block: `meanV` uses the section's `ρ` and `φ`. (This was given as the reason `codeMeaning` ignored its assignment argument; it is not one, since `codeMeaning` is defined after the block and can pass its own: D18.)
- Revisit: `TyS.label` as a type component is a hack for carrying a borrow result's label; a `Res` structure with an explicit `label` field would be cleaner. Revisit: `⟦&A⟧ = ⟦A⟧` (D1) versus a wrapper type, if WF8's maintenance proves awkward. Revisit: Mathlib's `ZFSet` for an extensional model if carrier-equality casts dominate the step proofs.

### Milestone 3 plan (partition for provers; ~75 statements, 10 files)

Done: `Steps/Borrow.lean` (8), `Steps/Subst.lean` (8), `Steps/Eq.lean` (6), `Steps/Call.lean` (5), `Steps/Match.lean` (7: evalMatch, evalMatchInd, evalMatchByType, split_coverage, generalizeNeutral, closeBlock, exitWorld), `Steps/Obs.lean` (4: observe, footprint_coverage, idType, owners_sound), `Steps/Types.lean` (9: evalType, piSort_aligned, capture, evalCtor, evalTInd, fieldTypesOf, repackCheck_sound, bodyReturns_sound, valType_sound), `Steps/WF.lean` (8: clause-wise preservation per step family, types_holeFree). `Steps/Eval.lean` (6: evalCore_step, the induction step itself; letIn, eq, J both outcomes, proof positions, η at Unit), `Steps/Tail.lean` (5: generic_good, checkTail_step, checkFix_sound = the corollary, checkConst_sound, checkInd_sound), `Steps/Decisions.lean` (7: scopeOf_sstate, each `Decisions` field equals its `Decide` function, joinMoves_static). The skeleton is complete; provers partition by file and own only private auxiliaries. To write, one statement per checker function, each with `Runs`, `Good`, `Below`/`Exact`, `Good` kept: `Steps/Call.lean` (callFn untyped/typed, runBody, closeCall ×3 rows + η at `Unit`, callType/declCallType, paramTypes, recCheck's accepting direction = stage stabilisation, endBorrowArgs, wholeBorrowArgs: ~10); `Steps/Match.lean` (evalMatch, evalMatchInd, evalMatchByType, splitThenClose/splitArmsThenClose with coverage ("every assignment selects an arm whose refinement it satisfies"), generalizeNeutral (path condition), blockCaptures ⊑, closeBlock (a block is a call of an anonymous function: exact, with D84's join), enterWorld/exitWorld (X1–X4: the restored state's meaning is the snapshot's): ~9); `Steps/Obs.lean` (observe = `observation`, idType = the `.id` clause, footprint coverage: positions outside `Obs.footprint` agree on both sides, owners: ~4); `Steps/Types.lean` (evalType, piSort = `Decisions.piSort`, capture (running the rewritten closure body with captures bound = running the original in the forming frame), evalTInd/evalCtor = the `.tind`/`.ctor` clauses, fieldTypes/fieldTypeAt = `fieldTy`, repackCheck/repackAt/bodyReturns establish membership under every future, lentUnknown, valType? soundness: ~10); `Steps/Eval.lean` (evalCore per remaining form: place/borrow/assign/letIn/seq/const/val/sort/pi/fix/succ/eq/ref/J/prims/ascribe, each = the run's clause: ~12); `Steps/Tail.lean` (checkTail/checkTailAt per tail rule, checkFix = Corollary "an accepted definition means an element of its type" per definition, checkDef, checkInd = (I1)–(I9) imply the data model exists: ~6); `Steps/WF.lean` (preservation of WF1–WF8 per step family: ~8); `Steps/Decisions.lean` (each `Decisions` field equals the checker's `Decide` function: ~6).

### Milestone 4 (stated in `Ochr/Meaning/Consistency.lean`)

The chain: (1) `accepted_resolves`: `Accepted` means every declaration resolves to an item and `checkItem` accepts each from the Σ of the earlier ones (`Program.progMeaning_sig` ties `progMeaning` to `globalsAfterFrom`); (2) `defn_member`: every accepted definition's meaning is a member of its type (`Steps/Tail.checkFix_sound`, `checkConst_sound`, `generic_good`, `checkInd_sound`); (3) `false_means_empty`: the library's `False`, with no constructors, means `prop False` (`pack_fields`); `falseConst_ty`: a parameterless `: False` definition is stored at that type; (4) `consistency_rel`: relative to a data model covering the program; `exists_dataModel` (the generic construction) and `consistencyBounded_assembled`. Covers `Type₀`- and `Prop`-sorted definitions as the run stands (D12). `#print axioms` on the assembled theorem must end at `sorryAx` plus the standard three once the chain is filled; the target `Ochr.Consistency.consistencyBounded` in `Bounded.lean` then takes `consistencyBounded_assembled` as its proof.
